## Context

The operator requires an agent self-review and a hostile validation (HV) on every result, plus truthful, per-request audit in the MCP session log. Today:

- Reviews have no structured home. They live in free-text turns, `docs/receipts/hv/*` (integer `accuracy`/`completeness` 0-100), and the unlinked HostileReview subsystem (`double` 0-1). No score in the codebase is a lossless decimal.
- Turn logging is not trustworthy, so anything attached to a turn would attach to the wrong request. Receipts observed in this session (plugin 1.118.0):
  - Twin turns per prompt (e.g. `req-20261009T011653Z-prompt-b776` and `-c9a3`): MCP hooks were then registered twice (`~/.claude/settings.json` bridge hooks plus the enabled plugin `mcpserver@mcpserver-local` `hooks/hooks.json`). The bridge hooks were removed later on 2026-10-09 (MEMORY-FACT-003); a read-only check at 2026-10-09T17:54Z found no `hooks` key in `~/.claude/settings.json` or either `settings.local.json`, one plugin install, and one registration per event. The defect that let the duplicate go unreported remains: `validate-claude-mcp-hooks.ps1` wraps the `enabledPlugins` map as one item (line ~72), so it never sees the plugin as enabled and would report `valid` for a duplicate. A2 is therefore a regression fix verified against a fresh live inventory, not a cleanup of a current duplicate. Observed later the same day with hooks active: the prompt hook opens phantom turns for background task notifications, and each such open supersede-cancels the real turn in progress (`req-20261009T175347Z-prompt-dc63`, `req-20261009T175421Z-prompt-79dd`).
  - A new prompt cancels the in-progress turn: `Invoke-ReplSupersedeCurrentTurnIfInProgress` (`plugins/core/lib-ps/repl-invoke.ps1:2131`, called at `:2300`). Canceled while being worked: `6067`, `5360`, `bc34`, `8ab5`, `11dd`, `ea25`, `9a7d`, and the plan request `e18e`.
  - Background `<task-notification>` events open turns (`-986f`, `-06ab`, `-290b`, `-4ced`, `-695f`).
  - False durable-write receipt: `appendActions` on `req-20261009T005031Z-prompt-d3a5` returned `code: persisted, persisted: true`, but the server turn has 0 actions. The submit terminal guard `continue`s silently (`src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/SessionLogService.cs:1606-1618`) while the controller hard-codes `persisted = true` (`SessionLogController.cs:71-86`).
  - The stop gate auto-closes turns as `'Auto-closed by PowerShell stop gate.'` (`plugins/core/lib-ps/plugin-hook.ps1:1017`).
  - A single shared `current-turn.yaml` couples all turn edits, so edits to one request land on another.
  - Cross-session interference: this session's UserPromptSubmit hook reported `req-20261009T012640Z-prompt-b2ff` (a turn of a different ClaudeCode session, likely the Plan subagent's prompt) as "the root work turn"; hooks of subagents and parallel sessions share the workspace cache.
  - Hook-current turns can exist only locally (`req-20261009T013814Z-prompt-7ee0`: server PATCH returned "planFile is omitted", turn absent server-side).

- Agents carry conversation history forward between invocations, so what a model knows depends on what happened to stay in its window: the operator profile, standing rules, applicable requirements, and earlier verified results are not guaranteed to be present, and nothing records which context an answer was produced from (operator-attached plan "Stateless Agent Context Management", 2026-10-11).

Intended outcome: every user request is exactly one turn whose edits are independent of every other turn; every write receipt is truthful; every turn carries a `requestClass` and two review records (`agentReview`, `hostileReview`) with lossless decimal scores, a status lifecycle, and configurable pass thresholds; the server enforces completion rules; every agent invocation can run stateless from a bounded, reproducible context package whose provenance is recorded, and only responses graded at 98/98 with zero FAIL and zero UNKNOWN are reused as trusted context; FR/TR/TEST/AC/UC are complete and traceable.

Changes to existing behavior: no phase is purely additive, Phase C included (architecture section 3, "Changes to existing behavior", and section 8.1, "Published packages"). Phase C adds tables, endpoints, and the `Mcp:AgentContext:Mode` setting, but the shared components it brings change what every existing caller sees in every Mode: `IDispatcher` in the published `QBrainAI.Cqrs` package becomes scoped and WorkspaceResolutionBehavior runs on every command and query; a deadlock or busy store that outlasts SerializableWrite's attempts answers 409 `conflict` with `retryable: true` where develop answers 503; and the published `QBrainAI.Client` package gains public API. Phases A and B change turn identity, turn writes, and the plugin's per-turn store. "Breaking changes, rollout, backfill" lists each change to existing behavior once, and each domain's "Updated existing requirements" and "Test churn" carry the records and tests it changes.

## Locked operator decisions (2026-10-09)

Design decisions: the turn-logging decisions (D1, D2, D5 to D9, D11, D12, plugin turn independence, the unsupported-session failsafe, the clear-session closing rule), the request class, the review record, and the completion rules are defined in the architecture document section 3.1, with their verbatim operator quotes.

Scope and process
- Server + client + REPL + MCP tools + plugin core, and every agent plugin updated and tested as needed (matrix below), in three scope areas: A (turn logging), B (class and reviews), C (stateless agent context), delivered through the four execution phases of "Execution organization" (requirements; interfaces and code stubs; TDD unit tests with mocks; implementation by bounded context with parallel coding agents).
- Strict BDPv4 per work package (below). Complete FR/TR/TEST with ACs and Use Cases (UC) for every new FR.
- Hostile validation runs on Codex CLI with model `gpt-6-astra` at reasoning effort `max` (operator, 2026-10-12: "I want the HV executed by Astra 6.1 with the highest level of effort.", resolved with the operator as model `gpt-6-astra` at effort `max`; it replaced `gpt-6-sol` at `xhigh`), launched by the implementing agent at the HV points of "Execution organization" (each prerequisite, the end of Phase 1, Phase 2, and Phase 3, each bounded context at the end of its Phase 4 work package, and the phase-wide Phase 4 HV), each once all of its gates are green (operator D10, 2026-10-09: "Only HV when all green at end of slice", applied to these work packages). This replaces the earlier PR-comment HV protocol (operator, 2026-10-09: "Yes, replace the PR-comment HV with Codex gpt-6-sol xhigh").
- Licensing (operator, 2026-10-09: "preserve license on existing files, Apache 2.0 for new files."): existing files and existing package declarations keep their license unchanged (root `LICENSE` Apache-2.0; existing `PackageLicenseExpression` MIT values; `plugins/core/lib-node/package.json` MIT); every file this plan creates in McpServer or a plugin repo is Apache-2.0 and, where the file type allows comments, starts with an `SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` comment; any new package or artifact manifest declares Apache-2.0. Each HV point's self-review, P0's included, records `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=A <base>..HEAD` per changed repo with, per file, its SPDX line or "no comment syntax", and any added commentable file without `SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` is a FAIL claim in that HV (triage `triage-report-8509a6345e6c408a981ade489513fa2b` records the pre-existing MIT/Apache mismatch).

Their host bindings (nonce source, host session key, first-ingress session id, stop-equivalent, child-session marker, single-registration check) are PowerShell core code delivered in A8 and tested with Pester through recorded host payload fixtures; their review and class surfaces are S8's, exposed to them in S9. The TypeScript-host adapter contract (operator, 2026-10-11, PowerShell-only ruling) is defined in the architecture document section 3.2, with its Pester cases.

Installed equals synced: a sync changes the plugin checkouts (each work package's G6 tests those checkouts) and ends by writing them into the installed host caches of the synced version (`RefreshKnownPluginCaches`, `build/Build.SyncAgentPlugins.cs:71,652-662` at `a8d3934b`), so no gate runs the sync: it runs only at the installed-equals-synced step after the server deploy, which its own cache refresh begins, and as a same-version re-sync (architecture sections 8.4 and 8.5). Each host runs the copy it installed: Codex the copy its Git marketplace installed (`[marketplaces.mcpserver-codex-plugin]`, source `https://github.com/sharpninja/mcpserver-codex-plugin.git`, cached under `~/.codex/plugins/cache/mcpserver-codex-plugin/mcpserver/<version>`), and Claude Code the copy cached from its directory marketplace (`mcpserver@mcpserver-local` from `/home/sharpninja/github/mcpserver-claude-code-plugin`, under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/mcpserver-local/mcpserver/<version>`). A host's installed copy is refreshed only after the server its synced plugins call is deployed (plugins never ship ahead of the server they call): in the Phase 4 live checks after their deploy (from the pushed plugin branches, 1.120.0) and in the post-merge rollout after its deploy (from the merged default branches). Between those points every host keeps its installed copy, so every HV reviewer before the Phase 4 live checks uses the pre-plan Codex install (it needs no class or review, because the completion gate is not deployed), and the implementing host's 1.120.0 verbs never run against a server without review records (the Phase 4 deploy carries TurnReviews before any plugin is refreshed). The refresh covers every host whose live check, live smoke, or HV reviewer runs on this machine, through that host's plugin manager, and removes before it installs, because an install of the same version (as after a merge) may not replace the cached copy (Codex: `codex plugin marketplace remove mcpserver-codex-plugin`, `codex plugin marketplace add https://github.com/sharpninja/mcpserver-codex-plugin.git --ref <branch>`, `codex plugin remove mcpserver@mcpserver-codex-plugin`, `codex plugin add mcpserver@mcpserver-codex-plugin`, then `codex app-server daemon restart` so daemon-hosted sessions load the refreshed copy (run only with operator approval, because it restarts the server that hosts the interactive Codex sessions on this machine); Claude Code: `claude plugin marketplace update mcpserver-local`, `claude plugin uninstall --keep-data mcpserver@mcpserver-local`, `claude plugin install mcpserver@mcpserver-local`, then a restart; for a host the sync installs directly, such as cline-v2 under `~/.cline/plugins/_installed/local`, the sync is that step). Its receipt records per host that the installed version and revision equal the sync receipt's version and the branch head and that the installed `lib/plugin-hook.ps1` (codex also `hooks/hooks.json`; a TypeScript host also its adapter entry file and `lib/tool-descriptors.json`) has the synced SHA-256. No trust step, live check, live assertion, or HV launch that depends on a refresh proceeds without this receipt.

## Execution organization (operator, 2026-10-11): four phases, then parallel domain coding

Operator directive (verbatim): "I want the implementation plan to be organized into requirements as a phase, interfaces and code stubs as a phase for the entire plan, test-driven development unit tests with mocks as a single phase, and then you can begin implementation coding in a domain-driven design breakdown so that agents can be run in parallel for the coding."

Architecture section 8 defines this delivery structure (8.1 phase order and published packages, 8.2 what each phase delivers, 8.3 bounded contexts and their merge order, 8.4 gates, 8.5 rollout and rollback); this section schedules it, cites it, and adds no delivery rule that contradicts it. Precedence: within the plan, this section fixes the execution order, phase gates, hostile-validation points, and TODOs for the whole plan. The sections "Scope catalog: Phase A", "Scope catalog: Phase B", and "Scope catalog: Phase C" (formerly the per-slice phases) and the P0 steps define scope, contracts, named tests, gates, and acceptance only; wherever they describe per-slice ordering, per-slice TODO transitions, or per-slice HV, this section governs. Each slice text is delivered through the phases below: its "Contract" part in Phase 2, its "RED" and "Mocks-green" parts in Phase 3, and its "Real" part, integration gates, and live checks in Phase 4 under the bounded context that owns its files.

Analyzer merge first (architecture section 8.1): the operator-directed analyzer branch `chore/netanalyzers-all` merges before the prerequisites and Phase 1 (code freeze exception), so the P0.1 baseline, every Phase 2 type, and every Phase 3 test name are taken under its rules (IDE0130, MA0048, CA1707) and at its paths (`build/Build.Tests`). It is not work of this plan and has no TODO or HV point in it; P0.1 records its merge commit on `develop`.

Prerequisites (after the analyzer merge and before Phase 1, each under its own TODO and the BDPv4 loop, then their own HV; architecture section 8.2): the P0.1 baseline (every pre-existing failure fixed, zero failures and zero skips, including the Windows findings), P0.0a (REPL `generateDocument` validator), P0.1a (Nuke `Test` parameters, which also reach the integration and validation projects of G3, `TestPluginCore`, `TestAgentPlugins`; TR-MCP-NUKE-011..013), P0.1b (bats cases ported to Pester, `MAINT-BATSTOPESTER-001`; operator, 2026-10-11, PowerShell-only ruling), P0.1c (`build/Build.Tests` removed from `MigrationIntegrationTests`, TR-MCP-NUKE-014; the analyzer branch moved the project and still lists it there, `build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:73` at `fe5879ba`), and the named prerequisites BUG-BUILD-005 (`ConfigValidator` and the `QBrainAi:` root, fixed before the first G5 run) and BUG-BUILD-008 (landed at plugin version 1.119.0 before this plan's sync at 1.120.0), because every later gate runs through those Nuke targets and Pester is the only plugin test runner (architecture section 8.4).

Phase 1: Requirements (entire plan). P0 steps 1-9 as written, with the TODO list of P0 step 8 replaced by the phase and domain TODOs below. Output: every FR, TR, TEST, AC, mapping, and use case of the catalog (Phases A, B, and C, organized by problem domain) in the store and in this plan; the agent-context catalog completed from the architecture document section 6; the feature matrix rows from P0.2. Exit: G5 phase-exit rule, then the phase HV of requirements, then operator approval.

Phase 2: Interfaces and code stubs (entire plan). Every contract named by the scope catalog and the architecture document, as compiling code whose members return neutral values (empty, null, false; never throwing) so Phase 3 tests fail on assertions:
- CQRS: every command and query record and its handler stub (one per unit of work, the record and its handler in two files, MA0048, in the folders and namespaces architecture section 8.3 names), registered by `AddCqrsHandlers`; every policy interface and stub (TerminalTurnOutcomeGuard through `ITerminalTurnOutcomeGuard`, RequestClassification through `IRequestClassNormalizer`, ReviewValidation through `ITurnReviewValidator`, CompletionPolicy through `ICompletionPolicy`, HvVerdictMapping, CompletionGate; architecture sections 4.3 and 5.3, "Policy seams (S1)"); every section 3.5 component with its Phase 2 seam and a stub (for example `TurnLocator`, `TurnWritePipeline`, `SerializableWrite`, `IdempotentCreate`, `ReadableKeyAllocator`, `PagedQuery`, `SecretRedactor`, `ContentHasher`, `ScoreLexeme` with `ScoreLexemeJsonConverter`, `ScoreVerdict`, `ReviewDefaults`, `SourceResolver`, `BuiltPackageLookup`, `IResponseStalenessRule`, `RelationshipWriter`), and the two CQRS behaviors (WorkspaceResolutionBehavior and ModeGateBehavior) registered in both composition roots as pass-through stubs that call the next handler, while `IDispatcher` keeps today's singleton registration (`src/QBrainAi.Cqrs/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:24-25` at `a8d3934b`) until SharedComponents changes it in Phase 4, so existing behavior stays live (architecture section 8.2).
- Existing behavior stays live through Phases 2 and 3 (round-4 finding R4-F10): the handlers of the units that wrap today's routes (SubmitSessionLogCommand, UpsertSessionTurnCommand, PatchSessionTurnCommand, ReplaceSessionTurnCommand, CompleteSessionTurnCommand, FailSessionTurnCommand, OpenSessionLogCommand, AppendTurnDialogCommand, SetTurnTitleCommand, SetSessionTitleCommand, GetSessionLogQuery, QuerySessionLogsQuery, and the section routes for every non-review section) are delegating handlers, not stubs: each calls today's `ISessionLogService` member unchanged, so every existing route, MCP tool, REPL method, and plugin path keeps today's behavior and the existing suites (for example `SessionLogControllerReceiptTests`) stay green unchanged. Only the new members those handlers will call (`SubmitWithOutcomesAsync`, the guard seam, the review and gate seams) and the new units (the mint, the review sections, every agent-context unit) are stubs, reached by no existing path. Each owning Phase 4 context switches its delegating handlers to the new members when its Phase 3 tests go green (its activation point), and the existing regression suites stay green at every gate, except the existing tests whose outcome a Phase 4 change deliberately changes: the change that causes the new outcome migrates or rewrites them as a step of its own gate, never as an exclusion (architecture section 8.4, test churn; each domain's "Test churn" names the tests, the change, and the owning TEST record).
- Shared files that parallel agents must not edit later, completed here in full: new entity classes and their `IEntityTypeConfiguration<T>` classes as compiling, unregistered seams (no `DbSet`, no registration in `McpDbContext`, and no new property on an existing entity: `McpDbContext.cs:301-308` ignores the pending-model-changes warning, so a registered model change without its migration would make queries select columns the database lacks and regress the existing tests that call `Migrate()`, 62 call sites in `tests/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Tests`; the registered model change, the migrations, and the S3 backfill are real schema behavior and belong to the Phase 4 context StorageSchema, after their Phase 3 RED tests); `McpClientJsonContext` registrations; DTOs and their client mirrors; `GenericClientPassthrough` allow-list, `ClientMutationPolicy`, and `ReplYamlMessageValidator` registrations; `ClientMutationPolicy`'s agent-context entries with the `Evaluate` change they need, which drops the `!ReadMethods.ContainsKey(clientName)` clause at `src/QBrainAi.Repl.Core/ClientMutationPolicy.cs:213` at `a8d3934b` (no namespace is in both lists today, so no existing decision changes and the existing policy tests stay green; architecture section 3.1, "Transaction-gated REPL clients"); DI in `Program.cs` and `McpStdioHost.cs`; `appsettings.yaml` sections and options classes with validation, `AgentContextOptions` included; the `agent_context` federation domain registration (FR-MCP-103, a change that completes behavior, so its case is recorded red first, Phase 3 below); the dispatch of EvaluateStalenessCommand from the requirement write paths of `RequirementsDatabaseDocumentService` in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Requirements/` after their commit, made only while Mode is not `Off` (architecture section 3.1, "Requirement writes and staleness"); the surface error mapping (next bullet); the golden contract files `docs/context/session-turn-review-contract.json` and `docs/context/agent-context-contract.json` (architecture section 8.3, ownership rule).
- Surfaces complete (they only dispatch): controllers and routes with their aliases, MCP tools, typed client methods, REPL methods; each controller route and MCP tool complete with its unit's error mapping, which maps every typed failure of the unit to the bodies of its error list (today's body where the unit keeps it, otherwise the `ClassifiedError` body), with one golden case per route in Phase 3 (RED against the stubbed handler, green against a mocked dispatcher). The owner of the surface error mapping is this Phase 2 work package (`DESIGN-MERGEDPLAN-001`): no Phase 4 context edits a surface file, and a context whose unit needs another mapping raises a Phase 2 amendment (architecture section 8.3, ownership rule); plugin function and tool signatures in `plugins/core/lib-ps` only (new PowerShell functions returning `$null`, `$false`, or `@()`, called by no existing hook or tool until Phase 4; existing core functions keep today's behavior); no plugin repo changes before Phase 4 (round-4 finding R4-F11): every host keeps its live glue, the TypeScript hosts their current adapters and Jest suites, and in Phase 4 HostPluginAdoption replaces each TypeScript host's adapter with its forwarding form and its Jest suite with its Pester suite in one change, after PluginCorePowerShell and the one sync (A9), so no phase ever runs a TypeScript host without its test suite or against an absent core; Nuke target skeletons named by the catalog.
Exit: `./build.ps1 Compile` on the merged analyzer configuration (IDE0130, MA0048, and CA1707 under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, so every new type and test name follows them), G2 shows no regression of existing tests (the delegating handlers keep them green unchanged), G4 green for the existing core suites, G6 unchanged because no plugin repo changes before Phase 4 (the TypeScript hosts stay outside the Pester baseline until A9, as P0.7 records), the stubs listed against the catalog, then the phase HV of interfaces, then operator approval.

Phase 3: Test-driven unit tests with mocks (single phase, entire plan). Every unit test the scope catalog names, written from the ACs, run RED against the Phase 2 stubs (the RED receipt: command, counts, first assertion per test class), then made green against mocks only (contract tests with an oracle double and permanent mutant tests; NSubstitute or hand-written doubles; Pester `Mock`), each with its recorded negative check (a wrong mock fails the target test). Each section 3.5 component gets its one test class (for example `SerializableWriteTests`), and a unit's tests mock the component's Phase 2 seam and never test the component's own branches again (architecture section 8.2); every new case name is PascalCase without underscores (CA1707), and the RED receipt maps every case the catalog names to a test the run discovered (the Nuke `Test` listing and Pester discovery), with none missing. A Phase 2 change that completes behavior rather than stubbing it (the `agent_context` federation registration, FR-MCP-103-AC006) records its case red in Phase 2, before the change, and Phase 3 keeps it green (architecture sections 3.1, "Test placement", and 8.2). Also the golden-contract tests, one golden case per surface route, and the documentation contract tests. Integration suites (G3), `PluginSessionLogIntegration`, and live checks are not part of this phase. Exit: every Phase 3 test green against mocks with zero skips, the RED and negative-check receipts, then the phase HV of tests, then operator approval.

Phase 4: Implementation by bounded context, coding agents in parallel. Each context below is one work package with one coding agent, its own branch from the integration head (the Phase 3 head plus every context merged before it; architecture section 8.3, process RunDomainCodingAgent), and its own TODO; a context starts when every context it depends on has merged (architecture section 8.3 merge order), and contexts run in parallel where that order allows. Because Phase 2 completed every shared file, each agent edits only the files its context owns; an agent that needs a change outside its context stops and raises it for a Phase 2 amendment. Each agent turns its context's Phase 3 tests green against the real implementation (no test weakened), adds its integration tests, runs its gates with zero failures and zero skips, and ends with its own context HV. After all contexts merge: G1-G6 on Linux (LAB-OMARCHY) and Windows (PAYTON-LEGION2 through the WSMan route), `PluginSessionLogIntegration`, the live checks of "End-to-end verification", the phase-wide HV, and operator approval.

Bounded contexts (owned files, dependencies, catalog content):
- StorageSchema (server, serialized). Owns the registered `McpDbContext` model change (the new `DbSet`s and configuration registrations, the new properties on `SessionLogTurnEntity`, and the `ResolveWorkspaceIdForAddedEntity` cases), the dump policy of each new `DbSet` in `WikiDumpTablePolicyRegistry` (FR-MCP-WIKIEXPORT-003, in the same change as the sets, because `tests/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Tests/Services/WikiDumpG7OverlayTests.cs:56-71` at `a8d3934b` requires a policy for every registered set), the migrations `<utc>_AddSessionLogTurnIngressNonce`, `<utc>_AddSessionLogTurnReviewsAndRequestClass`, and `<utc>_AddAgentContext` for SQLite, PostgreSQL, and SQL Server, generated in that order so the model snapshots stay linear, and the S3 backfill (`RequestClass` `Unclassified`, both review statuses `Unstarted`, soft-deleted rows included) with their migration and backfill tests. It runs first in Phase 4, alone, and merges before any other server context starts, because every migration edits the shared model snapshot and SharedComponents' three-provider cases use the registered model. Delivers the schema parts of A1, S3, and AgentContextStorage: every registered-model, migration, and backfill item in those scope entries' Contract parts is delivered here in Phase 4, never in Phase 2. Depends on: Phase 3.
- SharedComponents (server and `QBrainAi.Cqrs`, serialized, second). Owns the components of architecture sections 3.5.1 and 3.5.2 and their folders: the `QBrainAi.Cqrs` dispatch scope (the scoped `IDispatcher` facade in `src/QBrainAi.Cqrs/`, published as `QBrainAI.Cqrs`, architecture section 8.1), `WorkspaceResolutionBehavior` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/Workspaces/`), `ModeGateBehavior` and `IModeGatedRequest` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/`), `DbErrorClassifier`, `SerializableWrite`, `IdempotentCreate` with `PayloadFingerprint`, `ReadableKeyAllocator`, and `PagedQuery` with `PageBounds` (`src/QBrainAi.Storage/Errors/`, `Transactions/`, `Idempotency/`, `Keys/`, `Paging/`), `PageResult<TItem>` (`src/QBrainAi.Client/Paging/`), `ClassifiedErrorException` and `ErrorCodes` (`src/QBrainAi.Client/Errors/`) with the one `ClassifiedErrorException` branch of `McpErrorClassifier` (`src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp/Services/McpErrorClassifier.cs:14` at `a8d3934b`) and the REPL classifier's pass-through of a server `ClassifiedError` (code, message, retryable, details) at every status (`src/QBrainAi.Repl.Core/ReplMcpErrorClassifier.cs:60-95` at `a8d3934b`, whose conflict branch forces `retryable: false` today and which passes a server code through only for a 503; architecture section 3.5.2), `SecretRedactor` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/Redaction/`), and `ContentHasher` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/Hashing/`), each with its one test class. Delivers the shared-platform domain with StorageSchema. It runs alone, right after StorageSchema and before every other server context and ReplAndAgents; its gate runs only the cases its own components complete (gate case placement below). Depends on: StorageSchema.
- SessionTurnLogging (server). Owns the section 3.5.3 components in `src/QBrainAi.Services/SessionLog/` (`TurnStatus`, `TurnLocator`, `TurnWritePipeline`, `TurnReadProjection`, and `ITerminalTurnOutcomeGuard` with the TerminalTurnOutcomeGuard implementation); `SessionLogService.cs`, including every review-related change in it and in `TransactionGatedSessionLogService.cs` (the S4 service wiring of the review seams, the review sections in `TurnSection` and `ParseSection`, default records at turn creation, the Chore flip, include chains, graph paths, and the S10 write-origin scope with the gate call next to `ValidateTerminalTurnCompliance`); the record and handler files in `src/QBrainAi.Services/SessionLog/Commands/` and `Queries/` for MintSessionTurnCommand, SubmitSessionLogCommand, UpsertSessionTurnCommand, PatchSessionTurnCommand, ReplaceSessionTurnCommand, CompleteSessionTurnCommand, FailSessionTurnCommand, OpenSessionLogCommand, AppendTurnDialogCommand, SetTurnTitleCommand, SetSessionTitleCommand, GetSessionLogQuery, and QuerySessionLogsQuery (each switched from its Phase 2 delegating form at its activation point); the minter; and the nine-entry `agent_plugins` marker contracts in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/MarkerFileService.cs` (A9 server part, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC005, TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-009 AC-004). Delivers the server parts of slices A1, S2, S4 (service wiring), S10 (origins). Depends on: SharedComponents.
- TurnReviews (server). Owns the section 3.5.4 components (`ScoreLexeme` and `ScoreLexemeJsonConverter` in `src/QBrainAi.Client/Scoring/`, `ScoreVerdict` in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Scoring/`, `ReviewDefaults` in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Reviews/`), the review mapper, validator, request-class normalizer, and threshold provider, the `SessionLogSanitizer` review paths (through `SecretRedactor`), the RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand and ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand record and handler files in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Reviews/Commands/`, and the RequestClassification, HvVerdictMapping, and CompletionGate implementations, with the CompletionGate enforcement change set kept separate (architecture section 8.5). Delivers slices S1 (behavior), S4, S5 (behavior), and S10 (the gate); the S1 review DTOs in `src/QBrainAi.Client/Models/SessionLog/` are Phase 2 shared files, S3's schema, migration, and backfill belong to StorageSchema, and the `ConfigValidator` threshold check belongs to BuildTooling. SessionTurnLogging calls these components and policies only through their Phase 2 seams, so the two contexts run in parallel. Depends on: SharedComponents.
- ContextPackages (server). Owns `SourceResolver` and `SourceKind` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Sources/`; `SourceResolver.ReadCommit` is the commit reader), `PackageAssembler`, `CandidateRanker`, and `BuiltPackageLookup` with the candidate sources, renderer, and token estimator (`src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Packages/`), and the record and handler files in `src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Packages/Commands/` and `Queries/` of BuildAgentContextPackageCommand, GetAgentContextPackageQuery, QueryAgentContextPackagesQuery, ReplayAgentContextPackageQuery, RetrieveAgentContextCandidatesQuery (relational path), and GetAgentContextStatusQuery; its secret filter is `SecretRedactor` (SharedComponents). Delivers the agent-context scope AgentContextPackages. Depends on: SharedComponents.
- ResponsesAndTrust (server). Owns `ResponseStatusTransition`, `IResponseStalenessRule` with its implementation (the Phase 2 seam ContextPackages and ContextGraph call read-only), `ResponseIdList`, the grader, and the receipt verifier (`src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Responses/`), and the record and handler files in `src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Responses/Commands/` and `Queries/` of SubmitAgentResponseCommand, GetAgentResponseQuery, QueryAgentResponsesQuery, RunDeterministicGradeCommand, RecordModelGradeCommand, DecideAgentResponseTrustCommand, RejectAgentResponseCommand, SupersedeAgentResponseCommand, EvaluateStalenessCommand, CreateAgentContextHvRequestCommand, GetAgentContextHvRequestQuery, QueryAgentContextHvRequestsQuery, and GetAgentContextPilotReportQuery. Delivers AgentContextResponsesAndTrust. Depends on: SharedComponents.
- ContextGraph (server). Owns `RelationshipWriter` and the GraphRAG retrieval path behind the retrieval seam ContextPackages calls (`src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Graph/`), and the record and handler files in `src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Graph/Commands/` and `Queries/` of CreateAgentContextRelationshipCommand, DeleteAgentContextRelationshipCommand, QueryAgentContextRelationshipsQuery, and ProjectAgentResponsesCommand. Delivers AgentContextRelationships and AgentContextGraphRag. Depends on: SharedComponents.
- ReplAndAgents. Owns the REPL lossless score normalizer (which calls `ScoreLexeme`) and workflow pre-check (S6), including `importRecovery`'s report of the turns the completion gate refuses (`src/QBrainAi.Repl.Core/SessionLogWorkflow.cs:326` at `a8d3934b`), the McpAgent fields and pre-check (S7), and the optional `requestClass`, `agentReview`, and `hostileReview` arguments of QBAgent's `mcp_session_turn_complete` (`src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp/Services/QuadBrainInternalToolExecutor.cs:314-328` at `a8d3934b`; architecture section 3.1, "Completion gate writers"). Depends on: SharedComponents.
- PluginCorePowerShell. Owns `plugins/core/lib-ps/*` behavior: arrival bridge, prompt hook, verb binding, truthful persist, failsafe and alias journal, Stop gate, clear-session coordinator, HV receipt conversion, advisory pre-check, stateless invocation, agent-context builders (slices A3-A8, S8, the S9 descriptors, and AgentContextPluginCore; the one plugin core for all nine hosts, the TypeScript hosts included, operator 2026-10-11 PowerShell-only ruling), including `lib/tool-descriptors.json`, the section 3.5.6 functions in `plugins/core/lib-ps/repl-invoke.ps1` (the dot-sourceable library `plugin-hook.ps1` dot-sources), the arrival bridge's own script entry, and the processes BindVerbToTurn and PersistTurnWriteWithReceipt (architecture section 3.1, "Plugin function home"); also `plugins/core/test-fixtures/*` (the core Pester suites and the recorded host payload fixtures), `plugins/core/hooks-templates/*` (the grok-bot wrappers, which the generator's host list lacks today, `plugins/core/hooks-templates/generate-wrappers.ps1:8` at `a8d3934b`, and the codex `session-end` wrapper, which its codex branch does not write today, `:24-34`; A9) and `tests/QBrainAi.PluginIntegration.Tests` (the `GrokBot` host kind, `PluginHostKind` and `PluginHostProcessAdapter`, and the rewritten canonical flow; A9 and architecture section 4.1), which run before the sync and the integration gates. It calls no C# component, so it runs from the start of Phase 4 in parallel with the server contexts and merges after the server deploy (rollout order below). Depends on: Phase 3.
- BuildTooling. Owns `build/*` changes not already in the prerequisites and the infrastructure suite `build/Build.Tests` (moved from `tests/Build.Tests` by the analyzer branch; Phase 3 writes its cases; the S9 clear-session parity evidence is not there but in PluginIntegration and each plugin repo's Pester `Skills.Tests.ps1`, architecture section 3.1, "Test placement"; a Build.Tests case that another context's change breaks, such as `Catalog_HasExactlyEightEnabledScenarios` (`build/Build.Tests/PluginSessionLogIntegrationTargetTests.cs:82` at `fe5879ba`, a Build.Tests name that keeps its spelling) with PluginCorePowerShell's `GrokBot` scenario, is fixed by that context under the amendment rule of architecture section 8.3): `SyncAgentPlugins` explicit version (`--plugin-version`, where develop computes the next minor, `build/Build.SyncAgentPlugins.cs:63` at `a8d3934b`), receipt, grok-bot, host name, the lib-ps sync to the TypeScript hosts in place of the Node-core pack, the open-turn cache guard `HasOpenPluginTurn` reading every per-host-session `turn-state.yaml` as well as the legacy file (`:666,696-726`), and `ValidatePluginPowerShellOnly` (TR-MCP-NUKE-003 AC-005: an extension rule over every tracked path and the content rule over today's packaged paths, `:405-433`; the sync runs the packaged-path check, `:244-245`, and the tracked-tree check of all nine repos runs at HostPluginAdoption's exit; A9 build parts), and the `ConfigValidator` thresholds under both configuration roots, canonical `QBrainAi:` first, after BUG-BUILD-005 (S4 build part); architecture section 8.4. Evidence through the Build.Tests infrastructure step (TR-MCP-NUKE-* only). It calls no C# component, so it runs from the start of Phase 4. Depends on: Phase 3.
- HostPluginAdoption. Owns the nine plugin repos' host glue, skills, and docs (A2, the hook validator and installer in the Claude plugin repo; A9 host parts, including each TypeScript host's adapter and its Pester suite; S8 and S9 skill text, the clear-session skill parity, AgentContextPluginCore host adoption), run as one agent per repo; its exit runs `./build.ps1 ValidatePluginPowerShellOnly --agent-plugin-parent /home/sharpninja/github` over the tracked trees of all nine repos (architecture sections 8.4 and 8.5). Depends on: PluginCorePowerShell, BuildTooling (the sync).
- Documentation. Owns `docs/` and `templates/` (architecture section 8.3): `docs/context/*`, `docs/MCP-SERVER.md`, `docs/AGENT-PLUGIN-FEATURE-MATRIX.md`, `templates/prompt-templates.yaml`, the architecture document and `docs/Stateless-Agent-Context-Management.md` updates (S11, AgentContextExit). Depends on: every other context.

Cross-context calls go through the callee's Phase 2 seam and never wait for the callee to merge (architecture section 8.3 lists them); a context that needs a change outside its own files stops and raises a Phase 2 amendment instead of editing another context's files.

Problem domains and their contexts (operator, 2026-10-13; each domain section of the catalog names its contexts in its "Bounded contexts" line, which governs if this list and that line differ): Turn arrival and binding: SessionTurnLogging, PluginCorePowerShell. Turn persistence, decision lists, and closing: SessionTurnLogging, PluginCorePowerShell. Request class and the completion gate: SessionTurnLogging, TurnReviews, ReplAndAgents. Turn reviews, scores, and thresholds: TurnReviews, ReplAndAgents. Plugin core runners and host adoption: PluginCorePowerShell, HostPluginAdoption. Context packages and token budget: ContextPackages. Responses, grading, trust, and response lifecycle: ResponsesAndTrust. Relationships, retrieval, and GraphRAG projection: ContextGraph. Agent-context surfaces, mode, and stateless invocation: ContextPackages, PluginCorePowerShell, ReplAndAgents. Shared platform: StorageSchema, SharedComponents. Build tooling: BuildTooling. In every domain, the Phase 2 work package (`DESIGN-MERGEDPLAN-001`) delivers the surfaces and shared files, Phase 3 (`TEST-MERGEDPLAN-001`) the unit tests with mocks, and Documentation the documentation updates; a domain's ACs are set satisfied by the TODOs of the contexts that deliver them (TDD AC allocation).

Gate case placement (architecture section 8.4): a context's gate runs only the cases that its own code and the contexts merged before it complete; a case that needs two contexts runs at the gate of the one that merges later in the rollout order, on its branch with the earlier one merged, so no gate lists a case owned by a later context. SharedComponents' exit runs its own components' cases green before any context that calls them starts: among them `SerializableWriteRetriesExhausted409ConflictRetryableNothingSaved` and `SerializableWriteForcedInterleavingTestEntityCommitsWhollyBeforeOrAfter` on SQLite, PostgreSQL, and SQL Server, the dispatch-scope cases `ExistingHandlersProductionPipelineBehaviorUnchanged`, `DispatchNestedSharesDbContextAndTransaction`, and `DispatchParallelRequestsTwoWorkspacesNoSharedDbContext`, and the workspace-resolution, IdempotentCreate, ReadableKeyAllocator, ContentHasher, and REPL classifier cases. `TurnWriteConcurrentDifferentTurnsSameSessionBothCommitNoConflictAnswer` runs at SessionTurnLogging's gate; `TurnWriteForcedInterleavingChoreFlipVsRunningWriteRunningKept` and `ReviewReasonRootIdsEqualItsTurnKey` at TurnReviews'; `TurnWriteForcedInterleavingReviewResetVsCompleteNeverClosedWithoutReview` and `TurnWriteForcedInterleavingClassChangeVsCompleteGateSeesCommittedClass` at the gate of TurnReviews' CompletionGate enforcement change set; `BuildOnSqliteHookWriteDuringRetrievalNotBlocked` at ContextPackages'; and `ReadableKeyAllocatorSuffixPast99WidensLatestGradeOrderKept` at ResponsesAndTrust's. Test churn is carried by the change that causes it, as a step of that change's gate and never as an exclusion (architecture section 8.4); each domain's "Test churn" lists the tests, what changes, and the owning TEST record.

Phase 4 integration and rollout order (architecture section 8.5; keeps the approved safety order): merge StorageSchema, SharedComponents, SessionTurnLogging, TurnReviews without the CompletionGate enforcement change set, ContextPackages, ResponsesAndTrust, ContextGraph, ReplAndAgents, each after its gates re-run green on the integration head (architecture section 8.3, RunPhase4WorkPackages); deploy the server (Mode `Off`: the plugins stay stateful, every gated agent-context command answers 409 `agent_context_disabled`, and the read-only agent-context queries answer); merge PluginCorePowerShell, BuildTooling, then sync all nine plugins once at one explicit version (`./build.ps1 SyncAgentPlugins --agent-plugin-parent /home/sharpninja/github --plugin-version 1.120.0`, after BUG-BUILD-008 has landed at 1.119.0, carrying the Phase A, B, and C plugin work; the sync's own refresh of the installed caches of that version begins the installed-equals-synced step, which the operator's codex hook trust follows; a same-version re-sync, which never bumps a version, runs only when a later Phase 4 fix changes `plugins/core/lib-ps`) and merge HostPluginAdoption, whose exit runs `ValidatePluginPowerShellOnly` over the tracked trees of all nine repos (after which `MAINT-LIBSHRETIRE-001`, and once the 2026-10-12 CONTRACT entry is ACKed or resolved `MAINT-LIBNODERETIRE-001`, may run); then merge and deploy the CompletionGate enforcement change set (TurnReviews keeps it as a separate change set so plugins never ship behind the server gate they call), whose deploy receipt lists the workspace's turns in progress at that moment (QuerySessionLogsQuery, status `in_progress`), whose completion the gate refuses until each carries the items it names, with nothing backfilled (architecture section 3.1, "Turns open at the CompletionGate deploy"); then Documentation; then the phase-wide checks.

Hostile validation points (Codex gpt-6-astra max, AGREE at accuracy and completeness of at least 98 with every claim PASS; receipts, session-log storage, and reviewer-turn rules as in the HV protocol): each prerequisite, the end of Phase 1, Phase 2, and Phase 3, each bounded context at the end of its Phase 4 work package, and the phase-wide Phase 4 HV. This replaces the per-slice HV points (HV-A1..HV-A9, HV-S1..HV-S11, and the per-slice agent-context HVs); operator D10's rule ("Only HV when all green at end of slice") applies to these work packages.

TODOs (P0 step 8 creates them; ids in the repository's `<SDLC-PHASE>-<AREA>-###` form; "domain TODO" in this plan names a Phase 4 context TODO `IMPL-<CONTEXT>-001`, and a problem domain is worked through the TODOs of the contexts its section names, with no TODO of its own): `REQ-MERGEDPLAN-001` (Phase 1), `DESIGN-MERGEDPLAN-001` (Phase 2), `TEST-MERGEDPLAN-001` (Phase 3), and for Phase 4 `IMPL-STORAGESCHEMA-001`, `IMPL-SHAREDCOMPONENTS-001`, `IMPL-SESSIONTURNLOGGING-001`, `IMPL-TURNREVIEWS-001`, `IMPL-CONTEXTPACKAGES-001`, `IMPL-RESPONSESANDTRUST-001`, `IMPL-CONTEXTGRAPH-001`, `IMPL-REPLANDAGENTS-001`, `IMPL-PLUGINCOREPOWERSHELL-001`, `IMPL-BUILDTOOLING-001`, `IMPL-HOSTPLUGINADOPTION-001`, `IMPL-DOCUMENTATION-001`, and `VERIFY-MERGEDPLAN-001` (Phase 4 integration, live checks, phase-wide HV). Chain (the merge order of architecture section 8.3): DESIGN on REQ; TEST on DESIGN; every IMPL except HOSTPLUGINADOPTION and DOCUMENTATION on TEST; SHAREDCOMPONENTS also on STORAGESCHEMA; SESSIONTURNLOGGING, TURNREVIEWS, CONTEXTPACKAGES, RESPONSESANDTRUST, CONTEXTGRAPH, and REPLANDAGENTS also on STORAGESCHEMA and SHAREDCOMPONENTS; PLUGINCOREPOWERSHELL and BUILDTOOLING on TEST only (they call no C# component); HOSTPLUGINADOPTION on PLUGINCOREPOWERSHELL, BUILDTOOLING; DOCUMENTATION on every other IMPL; VERIFY on every IMPL. The analyzer merge precedes the prerequisites and is not in this chain (operator-directed work outside this plan). The TODO id `TEST-MERGEDPLAN-001` (architecture section 8.2) is named only in TODO records and in this plan, never in a requirement record's text, because `./build.ps1 ValidateTraceability` reads every `TEST-` token of `docs/Project/Testing-Requirements.md` as a TEST id (`build/TraceabilityValidator.cs:15-16,41-48` at `a8d3934b`); the catalog's common rules say how the TEST records refer to it. The PowerShell-only ruling (operator, 2026-10-11) adds three TODOs outside that chain: `MAINT-BATSTOPESTER-001` (prerequisite P0.1b), `MAINT-LIBSHRETIRE-001` (on IMPL-HOSTPLUGINADOPTION-001, once no host or build file references `plugins/core/lib-sh`; it deletes `plugins/core/lib-sh` with its six `.js` helpers, `plugins/core/sync/sync-plugin-core.sh`, `plugins/core/sync/check-core-integrity.sh`, and `plugins/core/ci-templates/core-guard.yml`, and retires or rewrites the five core Pester cases that read lib-sh, `plugins/core/test-fixtures/pester/PluginPowerShellRuntime.Tests.ps1:252,410,419,2779,2901`, all paths at `a8d3934b`), and `MAINT-LIBNODERETIRE-001` (on IMPL-HOSTPLUGINADOPTION-001 and Codex's ACK of the 2026-10-12 PowerShell-only CONTRACT entry, or the operator's resolution of it; it deletes `plugins/core/lib-node` with its 13 jest files under `plugins/core/lib-node/tests/` and the Node-core build code in `build/Build.SyncAgentPlugins.cs`, and rewrites the G2 case that asserts the Node memory shim exists, `tests/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Tests/Memory/MemoryPluginSyncTests.cs:66-71` at `a8d3934b`). Each retirement TODO also records, through the plugin requirements route, the FR-MCP-140 and TEST-MCP-189 update that removes its retired marker resolver (`plugins/core/lib-sh/marker-resolver.sh`, `plugins/core/lib-node/src/discovery/marker-resolver.ts`) from their verifier list (plugin-host domain section). Each TODO carries the FR and TR ids of the catalog content it delivers and the allocated ACs. After its HV pass, recorded with `RecordValidationResultAsync` (result `pass`, which marks the TODO's own acceptance criteria satisfied, `TodoExecutionService.cs:716-720`, as the move from Validating to Complete requires, `:1245-1246`), its allocated requirement ACs are set satisfied in the store, and then the TODO moves to Complete.

## BDPv4 process contract (applies to every work package; the per-slice wording below is mapped to phases by "Execution organization")

Sources: `docs/Development-Process-draft-v4.md:76` (tests validated with mocks before real code); `docs/Byrd-Dev-Process-v4-Project-Management-Guide.md` 5.1 status machine, 5.2 handoff contract, 5.3 test gate, line 221 loop "Red -> mocks-green -> real Green -> refactor -> full unit gate -> Validating"; `skills/byrd-tdd-process/SKILL.md`.

Requirements DB is authoritative; `docs/Project/*.md` is a generated projection (regenerated, never hand-edited). This overrides the SKILL step 1 wording ("append entries to docs/Project").

Per slice, in order:
1. Contract first: interfaces, DTO members, and skeletons returning neutral values (empty/null/false, never throwing) so RED fails on an assertion. Compile-failure RED only for the first stub of a phase. PowerShell stubs return `$null`/`$false`/`@()`.
2. RED: write the named tests from the ACs; run; record command, `Failed: N, Passed: M, Skipped: 0`, and the first assertion message as a TODO TestDefined checkpoint.
3. MOCKS-GREEN: make the same tests pass against mocks only, no production logic. Pure seams use the CT pattern: abstract `XxxContractTests` with an oracle subclass `XxxContractTestsOracle` (hand-written double), a production subclass `XxxContractTestsProduction`, and permanent `XxxMutantDetectionTests` (a deliberately wrong double must fail every assertion); the subclass names carry no underscore, because CA1707 covers every identifier, test methods, test classes, and test-only subclasses alike (architecture section 3.1, "Analyzer rules"). Glue slices substitute the seams. Negative check every slice: a wrong mock value makes the target test fail (recorded). Tooling: NSubstitute (Support.Mcp.Tests, Repl.Core.Tests, Support.Mcp.IntegrationTests); hand-written doubles in Client.Tests (`MockHttpHandler`) and McpAgent.Tests; Pester `Mock` plus seams `MCP_PLUGIN_REPL_LOG`/`MCP_PLUGIN_REPL_RESPONSE` (`plugin-hook.ps1:216-227`) and `MCP_PLUGIN_PERSIST_LOG` (`repl-invoke.ps1:1624-1640`).
4. Agent self-review of RED and mocks-green (recorded on the slice's session turn as decisions; from the first deploy whose server stores review records, the Phase 4 live-check deploy, also as `agentReview`) before any production change. No hostile review before the slice is all green (D10).
5. REAL GREEN: production code; state which tests stay contract tests.
6. REFACTOR: delete the `Oracle` subclasses, keep mutant tests; clean production code.
7. Gates G1-G6 (zero failures, zero skips):
   - G1 targeted `./build.ps1 Test --test-projects <proj> --test-filter "<filters>"` (the Nuke parameters P0.1a adds under TR-MCP-NUKE-011; operator 2026-10-11: builds and tests run as Nuke targets, never `dotnet test` directly) and/or targeted Pester through `./build.ps1 TestPluginCore --pester-paths` or `./build.ps1 TestAgentPlugins --plugins` (TR-MCP-NUKE-012, -013). Every gate that this plan runs on Windows runs natively on PAYTON-LEGION2 through the operator's WSMan route in a worktree under `F:/GitHub/McpServer/.worktrees/`; every Linux gate runs natively on LAB-OMARCHY.
   - G2 `./build.ps1 Test` (it excludes `*IntegrationTests`, `*PluginIntegration*`, `*.Validation`, `*Review.Tests`, and `Build.Tests`, and filters out `Category=Integration`, `build/Build.Test.cs:16-24,50` at `a8d3934b`).
   - The Build.Tests infrastructure step (not part of G1 or G2; operator, 2026-10-10: "Build.Tests is infrastructure, not application and should not be treated as unit tests." and "NUKE is only accountable through TR."): `dotnet test build/Build.Tests/Build.Tests.csproj -c Debug` over the full project (the path after the analyzer merge, `build/Build.Tests/Build.Tests.csproj` at `fe5879ba`), zero failures and zero skips (no Build.Tests case reads `MCP_AGENT_PLUGIN_PARENT` at `a8d3934b`, and the S9 clear-session parity evidence lives in PluginIntegration, G3, and each plugin repo's Pester `Skills.Tests.ps1`, G6, never in Build.Tests; architecture section 3.1, "Test placement") (a slice may first run its own cases with a `--filter`, as S4 does, but only the full-project run satisfies the step), run at the slice gate after G2 by every slice that changes `build/` code and by every slice whose gate list names it; for such a slice, every rule of this plan that requires its G1-G6 green or re-run (D10, the move to Validating, rebase re-runs, phase exits) also requires this step. Its baseline follows the P0.1 rule: every failure, skip, or hang the full project shows on the approval HEAD predates this plan, is filed in triage, and is fixed under its own TODO before the first gate that needs it, because no exclusion list is allowed (on 2026-10-10: BUG-BUILD-005, `ConfigValidator` cannot parse the live `QBrainAi:` appsettings so `./build.ps1 ValidateConfig` fails, before P0.9's G5; BUG-BUILD-003, -004, -006, and -007 before the first gate that runs this step). Its results are never reported as unit or integration test results, and its cases are evidence only for the TR ACs that name them, never for an FR AC or a TEST record. `./build.ps1 MigrationIntegrationTests` (G3) runs the `Category=Integration` cases of Build.Tests today (`build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:70-74` at `a8d3934b`, and `:73` at `fe5879ba`, which lists `build/Build.Tests` after the move), which contradicts the ruling that Nuke never runs its own tests (operator, 2026-10-11: "Nuke does not run its own test."); prerequisite P0.1c removes `build/Build.Tests` from that target's project list (TR-MCP-NUKE-014) before the first G3 run, and from then on those cases run only in this direct Build.Tests step, whose full-project run already includes `Category=Integration`.
   - G3 after G2 green, the integration set the slice names, through Nuke only (architecture section 8.4): `./build.ps1 Test --test-projects <project> --test-filter "<filter>"` for `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.IntegrationTests`, `QBrainAi.SessionLog.Validation`, `QBrainAi.Repl.IntegrationTests`, and `QBrainAi.Acid.IntegrationTests` (no Nuke target runs these four today, because `Test` leaves out every `*.IntegrationTests` and `*.Validation` project and filters out `Category=Integration`, `build/Build.Test.cs:16-24,50` at `a8d3934b`; P0.1a's `--test-projects` also selects such a project and drops that category term for it, TR-MCP-NUKE-011 AC-001 and AC-003); `./build.ps1 MigrationIntegrationTests --skip InstallTestDependencies` for the three-provider cases (against the PostgreSQL and SQL Server endpoints P0.1 provisions through `MCP_TEST_POSTGRES_CONNECTION` and `MCP_TEST_SQLSERVER_CONNECTION`; `InstallTestDependencies`, on which the target depends, is Windows-only, `build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:45-65`); and `./build.ps1 PluginSessionLogIntegration` (from A9 on, after the branch REPL install that the rollout step describes).
   - Nuke rule for suites (operator rulings of 2026-10-11): every non-Build.Tests build and test in this plan runs through a Nuke target natively on Windows or Linux. Wherever this plan names a plugin suite command (`Invoke-Pester`, the only plugin test runner under the 2026-10-11 PowerShell-only ruling), that command is what the Nuke target executes, and the gate runs the target: G4 through `./build.ps1 TestPluginCore --pester-paths <paths>` (TR-MCP-NUKE-012) and G6 through `./build.ps1 TestAgentPlugins --agent-plugin-parent /home/sharpninja/github [--plugins <names>]` (TR-MCP-NUKE-013), both added in P0.1a; the deploy artifact comes from `./build.ps1 Publish`. Build.Tests is the single direct exception.

8. Agent self-review of the finished work package (its turn decisions; from the Phase 4 live-check deploy also `agentReview`); its phase or domain TODO moves to `Validating`; then its Codex HV runs at its HV point (D10, "Execution organization") and the TODO moves to Complete only after that HV returns an HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS).

Hostile validation protocol (operator directives 2026-10-09: first "at completion of major phases", then refined by D10 to the end of every work unit once its gates are green; since the 2026-10-11 four-phase organization the HV points are those of "Execution organization"; reviewer is Codex CLI, model `gpt-6-astra`, reasoning effort `max`, replacing the earlier PR-comment protocol):
- HV points are those of "Execution organization" (each prerequisite, the end of Phases 1, 2, and 3, each bounded context at the end of its Phase 4 work package, and the phase-wide Phase 4 HV); they replace the approved pre-merge per-slice schedule (HV-P0 at P0's end, HV-A1..HV-A9, HV-S1..HV-S11, and the phase-wide HV-A and HV-B), and "HV-P0" below names the Phase 1 HV. Every work package ends with its own HV once its gates are green (D10). Turns of a Code or Docs work package that complete before its HV lands (operator confirmed this interpretation of D10 on 2026-10-09: "agree") record `hostileReview` status `Other` with `otherNotes` "pending HV <gate>" from the first deploy whose server stores review records, the Phase 4 live-check deploy; when the HV lands, its verdict, mapped as FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC002 states (server-derived Pass or Fail from the scores, or `Other` with `otherNotes` naming the failed claims when a DISAGREE has scores that would derive Pass), with both receipt paths is recorded with `recordReview` onto every turn of that work package, each addressed by its original (`sessionId`, `requestId`) pair (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002) (reviews stay writable on completed turns), and the HV brief lists those turns. Before that deploy, every HV point (the prerequisites, Phases 1-3, and the Phase 4 context HVs, which run before their contexts merge) records its verdict on its coordinating turn (dialog and designDecisions with the receipt paths) and with `RecordValidationResultAsync`, and its TODO moves to Complete on its HV pass. After that deploy, and before the phase-wide Phase 4 HV reviewer is launched, the Phase 4 context verdicts are recorded with `recordReview` onto every turn of those contexts by original pair (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002), the phase-wide brief lists those turns with its own, and `VERIFY-MERGEDPLAN-001` moves to Complete only after `client.SessionLog.QueryAsync` shows every turn listed in that brief carrying its verdict; an interrupted backfill is re-run (identical writes are no-ops).
- At each HV point, once its gates are green, push the worktree branch (and the branch of each plugin repo the work package changed). Write the HV brief: the claims (with commands, paths, test counts, SHAs, session turn ids), the untrusted implementer receipt, the acceptance rule, mandatory surfaces A-D, the add-profile first action, the review-only scope, and the receipt paths.
- Launch the reviewer with `codex exec -m gpt-6-astra -c model_reasoning_effort="max" --sandbox workspace-write -c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true -C <worktree> --json -`, brief on stdin. The reviewer is review-only (it writes only under `docs/receipts/hv/` and to its own MCP session-log turn) and returns claim-by-claim PASS/FAIL/UNKNOWN, `OverallVerdict`, accuracy, completeness, findings, and a `=== VERDICT JSON ===` block. The implementing agent writes the brief and launches the run; it never edits the reviewer's output or verdict. Verify the live model and effort from the Codex session rollout (`turn_context` with `model=gpt-6-astra`, `effort=max`); a run on any other model or effort is not an HV. Review time is unbounded (operator, 2026-10-12: "Do not bound review time"): no timeout, kill, deadline, or wait limit on the reviewer, and no expected duration in a brief; the implementer checks the response stream until `turn.completed` arrives.
- Receipts: the runner writes `docs/receipts/hv/<utc>-<gate>.request.jsonl` (a meta object with the launch command, model, effort, head SHA, and brief SHA-256, plus the full brief body; built from PowerShell objects with `ConvertTo-Json -Compress`) and `.response.jsonl` (the reviewer's complete `--json` event stream), and, after the reviewer completes, `.rollout.jsonl` (a copy of the completed reviewer thread's Codex rollout file, which records its runtime `turn_context`; the agent-context `runtime_identity` check reads it). The reviewer writes `hostile-validator-<gate>-<utc>.md` and `.json`. All are committed on the branch only after every marker key value and every `apiKey`, `api_key`, or `X-Api-Key` value of 20 or more characters in them is replaced with `[REDACTED:api-key]`, no other byte changing, and a scan against the current markers' keys finds no match (the repository is public, and the reviewer reads the markers). Store the complete verdict in the MCP session log on the turn that launched the HV (the work package's coordinating turn) (ordered dialog parts that concatenate byte for byte to the markdown receipt, plus designDecisions, the jsonl paths, and the Codex rollout path); the reviewer also stores it on its own turn, through the supported Codex plugin wrapper (manual `workflow.sessionlog.*` verbs when hooks do not mint), and reads it back; this is mandatory at every HV point: an HV whose reviewer turn cannot be created, populated, completed, and read back is incomplete, its work package stays open, and the coordinator's turn never substitutes for it. From the deploy that carries the CompletionGate enforcement change set (in the Phase 4 rollout order, and every later deploy), the reviewer's own turn meets that gate like any other: the HV brief has the reviewer, through the installed 1.120.0 Codex plugin, set its turn's `requestClass` to `Chore` (a review writes receipts, not product code or documentation, so its hostile record becomes `Unnecessary`) and record its `agentReview` as `Other` with `otherNotes` naming its verdict receipt (a review turn's self-review is its own verdict), before it completes the turn. Because codex reports `turn_logging_unsupported` after a refresh of its installed copy (the installed-equals-synced step, Per-plugin changes) changes its hook definition, until the operator trusts that definition and the codex live assertion passes (host binding table), that trust step and the assertion run after each such refresh and before the next HV reviewer is launched; if they cannot pass, the HV is not launched and the operator is shown the blocker. From the Phase 4 live-check deploy (the per-turn recording start above), also record it with `workflow.sessionlog.recordReview kind: hostile` (with `requestJsonlPath`/`responseJsonlPath`).
- Pass only with `OverallVerdict AGREE` and accuracy >= 98 and completeness >= 98 and every claim PASS (no FAIL or UNKNOWN). A run that ends without a `=== VERDICT JSON ===` block is recorded as aborted (its receipts committed, not an HV) and a new round is launched. On DISAGREE or any other non-pass (including AGREE with a FAIL or UNKNOWN claim): display every FAIL/UNKNOWN to the operator, fix on the branch, push, and launch a new Codex round whose brief re-attacks the prior receipt; the work package stays open (and, at a phase-end HV, its phase).
- PRs: each prerequisite, each of Phases 1, 2, and 3, and each Phase 4 bounded context lands through its own PR (McpServer against `develop`; each changed plugin repo against its remote default branch, `main`, or `master` for opencode; cross-linked), which cites the HV receipt paths and verdicts. Merge a PR only after its HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS), operator approval, and the local gate receipts for the PR head (Nuke targets for McpServer, G6 for plugin repos; the phase HV receipt cites them). GitHub Actions is not a gate (operator rulings 2026-10-11: "GitHub actions must not be considered part of any requirement or process." and "You must remove all Github Actions related reqs, tests and scripts."). The next phase starts only after its predecessor has an HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS), operator approval, every PR of that phase merged (McpServer and each changed plugin repo), and the merged heads verified on their target branches (operator phase rule, `MEMORY-PROCESS-006`); the prerequisites likewise complete before Phase 1 starts, and the Phase 4 context PRs merge in the rollout order of "Execution organization".

TODO transitions (plugin todo workflow / `client.Todo.*`; the rules of `TodoExecutionService.cs:1208-1257`: entering Implementing needs `unitTestsDefined`; TestDesign -> TestReady needs `unitTestsDefined`; Implementing -> Validating needs an `ImplementationProgress`, `CommitCreated`, or `TestPassing` checkpoint; Validating -> Complete needs `unitTestsPassing`, passing integration tests when defined, a passing validation, and every TODO acceptance criterion satisfied; a Blocked TODO resumes only with a reason; Complete reopens only to Planned or TestDesign). Each phase and domain TODO of "Execution organization" names the tests that count for it: `REQ-MERGEDPLAN-001` the G5 run and the documentation contract tests; `DESIGN-MERGEDPLAN-001` `./build.ps1 Compile`, G2 with no regression, and the golden-contract parse; `TEST-MERGEDPLAN-001` the Phase 3 suites (RED, then green against mocks with negative checks); each `IMPL-<CONTEXT>-001` its context's Phase 3 tests green against the real implementation plus its integration tests; `VERIFY-MERGEDPLAN-001` the full G1-G6, `PluginSessionLogIntegration`, and the live checks. Paths: REQ, DESIGN, IMPL, and VERIFY TODOs go Planned -> TestReady (`SetTestPlanAsync unitTestsDefined: true` naming those tests) -> Implementing -> Validating (a `TestPassing` checkpoint carrying the green receipt is appended first; at the gate `SetTestPlanAsync unitTestsPassing: true` and, for a TODO whose gates include G3, `integrationTestsDefined: true` and `integrationTestsPassing: true`) -> Complete; `TEST-MERGEDPLAN-001` goes Planned -> TestDesign -> TestReady (RED recorded) -> Implementing (mocks-green and negative checks recorded) -> Validating -> Complete. Every move to Complete happens only after that TODO's HV returns an HV pass (`OverallVerdict AGREE`, accuracy and completeness >= 98, and every claim PASS), recorded via `RecordValidationResultAsync` with the HV receipt paths and jsonl paths. A DISAGREE moves the TODO to Blocked with the findings, then fix and re-review; a phase-wide DISAGREE reopens every Complete TODO it names to TestDesign with the findings (Complete -> Blocked is not a permitted transition), and that TODO repeats its BDPv4 loop and its own HV.

Session logging during execution: every operator request is its own turn, targeted by explicit `requestId` (`client.SessionLog.BeginTurnAsync/PatchTurnAsync/CompleteTurnAsync`) until the Phase 4 deploy carries the turn-logging changes; decisions go to `designDecisions`; every write verified server-side with `client.SessionLog.QueryAsync`.

Toolchain gate on the execution host, recorded as a PlanningDecision: `dotnet --list-sdks` must include an SDK accepted by `global.json` (`10.0.201`, `rollForward: latestFeature`, so any 10.0.2xx or later feature band; on 2026-10-09 LAB-OMARCHY had only `10.0.111`, which fails `./build.ps1` with "A compatible .NET SDK was not found"), Pester 5 must be importable by `pwsh` (none installed on 2026-10-09), `node` must be present to run the TypeScript-host adapter tests under the hosts' own runtime (node v26.8.2 present; P0.7 records the runtime each TypeScript host's plugin API loads), and `dotnet ef` must run at the EF Core version pinned in `Directory.Packages.props` (`Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design` 10.0.0); on 2026-10-09 `dotnet ef --version` failed and `.config/dotnet-tools.json` pinned only `docfx`. Missing tools are installed only with operator approval and only through PowerShell: the SDK, on this Linux host, by a PowerShell-only download and extract pinned to 10.0.201: `Invoke-WebRequest` of `https://builds.dotnet.microsoft.com/dotnet/Sdk/10.0.201/dotnet-sdk-10.0.201-linux-x64.tar.gz`, a SHA-512 check against the `hash` that `https://builds.dotnet.microsoft.com/dotnet/release-metadata/10.0/releases.json` lists for that file (stop on mismatch), extraction with `tar -xzf` into `$HOME/.dotnet` invoked from PowerShell, `DOTNET_ROOT` and `PATH` set for the session, then `dotnet --list-sdks` showing 10.0.201 (Microsoft documents `dotnet-install.ps1` for Windows and `dotnet-install.sh` for Linux, so neither script is used here); `dotnet-ef` as a local tool pinned in the repo manifest (`dotnet tool install dotnet-ef --version 10.0.0` from the repo root, committed `.config/dotnet-tools.json`, then `dotnet tool restore`), invoked per provider as `dotnet tool run dotnet-ef migrations add <Name> --project src/QBrainAi.Storage.<Provider>Migrations --startup-project src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp --context McpDbContext` with `MCP_EF_PROVIDER` (`sqlite`, `postgresql`, `sqlserver`) and `MCP_EF_MIGRATIONS_ASSEMBLY=QBrainAi.Storage.<Provider>Migrations` read by `src/QBrainAi.Storage/McpDbContextFactory.cs:26-30` (form documented in `docs/Operations/permanent-local-audit-sqlserver.md:19-27`; `postgresql` is the canonical PostgreSQL name, `src/QBrainAi.Storage/Database/PostgreSqlMcpDatabaseProviderStrategy.cs:15`); `Install-Module Pester -MinimumVersion 5.0 -Scope CurrentUser`. P0.1 also provisions, with operator approval, PostgreSQL and SQL Server test endpoints on this Linux host as containers, setting `MCP_TEST_POSTGRES_CONNECTION` and `MCP_TEST_SQLSERVER_CONNECTION` (both honored by the provider tests), because `MigrationIntegrationTests` depends on `InstallTestDependencies` (`build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:45-65`), which provisions LocalDB with an elevated `msiexec` on Windows only (`build/Build.TestDependencies.cs:236-266`); every G3 use of the target therefore runs `./build.ps1 MigrationIntegrationTests --skip InstallTestDependencies`, and P0.1 records one such run against both endpoints. P0.1 also runs G2 (`./build.ps1 Test`) and `./build.ps1 PluginSessionLogIntegration` (zero skips) on the approval HEAD; every failure or skip they show (for example the AI lane's Windows `.cmd` strategy in `PluginSessionLogIntegration`) predates this plan, is filed in triage, and must be fixed under its own TODO before Phase 1 starts, because every gate requires zero failures and zero skips and no exclusion list is allowed (the same rule covers the G6 baseline that P0.7 runs after its fast-forward).
