Read-only spike (P0.2 writes nothing to the repo or the stores beyond its probe receipts, recorded payloads, and its PlanningDecision; the temporary codex observer files of (c) live outside the repo and are deleted at its end), recorded as a PlanningDecision: confirm from the session transcript and server turns how `<task-notification>` and slash commands arrive at UserPromptSubmit and whether each produces a `queue-operation` `enqueue` record; capture a Claude Code transcript excerpt with `queue-operation`/`enqueue` lines as the A3 arrival-bridge fixture (the bridge design does not depend on whether UserPromptSubmit also fires for queued messages, because on bridge hosts the prompt hook neither mints nor claims); and run every probe the host binding table assigns to P0.2, each recorded with its receipt, an outcome of pass, fail, or unavailable (host not installed or not reachable), and the resulting D6/D7 capability disposition (P0.8 writes it into `docs/AGENT-PLUGIN-FEATURE-MATRIX.md` from these receipts): (a) claude-code: whether every user prompt, in each prompt form named in FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC009, produces a transcript `enqueue` record, whether `dequeue` always takes the head of the queue, and whether a request's `dequeue` line (or its `remove` with `absorbed_mid_turn`) is in the transcript before that request's first tool call starts (otherwise claude-code is `turn_logging_unsupported`, D6; architecture section 3.1, "Late `dequeue` on claude-code"); (b) claude-cowork: whether Cowork executes plugin hooks, and the same bridge probes as (a) (every prompt form produces an `enqueue` record, `dequeue` always takes the head, delivery precedes the first tool call); Cowork is supported only if all pass, otherwise `turn_logging_unsupported` (D6) with its feature-matrix row; its row also records the target of the repaired connector, the PowerShell script `bin/mcpserver-stdio.ps1` started with `pwsh -File` (architecture section 3.1, "Host details"); (c) codex, observed in an isolated probe home, because the codex plugin registers no hooks before A9 (agent plugin matrix) and nothing of the operator's own Codex setup (`~/.codex` and the shared app-server daemon that hosts its interactive sessions) may change: P0.2 records the SHA-256 of `~/.codex/config.toml`, then creates `/tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/` holding `observer.ps1` (it reads the hook's stdin JSON and appends `{ observedAtUtc, payload }`, built from native objects with `ConvertTo-Json -Compress`, as one line to `observed.jsonl` in that directory, writes nothing to stdout, and exits 0) and `home/config.toml`. `home` is the `CODEX_HOME` of every probe command (codex-cli 0.160.1 reads its config and auth from `CODEX_HOME`); every probe command sets it with `env` from PowerShell, as the deploy step sets `PATH`; its config sets `approval_policy = "never"`, installs no plugin, and sets `enabled = false` (the `[plugins."<name>"]` key of the operator's own `config.toml`, not in the help output) for every bundled plugin whose manifest declares hooks (on 2026-10-10 `browser@openai-bundled`, `chrome@openai-bundled`, and `unified-computer-use@openai-bundled`), so the probe writes nothing to the MCP stores and the observer is the only enabled hook, and registers inline command hooks for SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse, and Stop (no matcher) that run the observer with `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive -File` (Codex hooks documentation, https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks: inline `[hooks]` tables in a config layer; a non-managed hook runs only once its definition is trusted). The operator signs in inside the probe home once (`env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex login`; the agent never handles the credentials). Run 1 (scripted): `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex exec --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust -s read-only -C /home/sharpninja/github/McpServer --json "Run the shell command printenv CODEX_THREAD_ID and reply with its output only."`, stdout kept; it passes when the observer recorded a UserPromptSubmit line whose payload carries a nonempty `session_id` and `turn_id`, and it records whether the printed `CODEX_THREAD_ID` equals that `session_id` (the `CODEX_THREAD_ID` verb key of the host binding table is selected only when the same equality also holds in run 2's app-server-hosted session, otherwise the live-session rule). Run 2 (the operator at the keyboard) hosts an interactive session on a private `codex app-server` started only for the probe (the program whose `daemon` subcommand manages the local app-server daemon, which hosts interactive sessions by default: `codex --help` says `--no-daemon` runs "without the shared background server"): in one terminal `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex app-server --listen unix:///tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/app.sock`; in a second, `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex --remote unix:///tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/app.sock -s read-only -a never -C /home/sharpninja/github/McpServer`, where the operator trusts the observer hooks with `/hooks` (Codex hooks documentation; that this works over `--remote` is untested, and if the hooks then do not run, run 2 cannot be performed) and sends prompt A `Run the shell command printenv CODEX_THREAD_ID, then the shell command sleep 90, then reply with the printenv output only` (run 2 records whether that output equals A's `session_id`); while A's `sleep 90` runs, one prompt B per way a message reaches a running session, each with its own text (`Reply with exactly: done B1`, `done B2`, and so on) so each observer line is attributable: each way the TUI offers for a message during a running turn (its on-screen hint names them), and, from a third terminal, `env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex queue --remote unix:///tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/app.sock --thread <A's session_id from the observer, used as the "Session UUID" that `--thread` takes; if `codex queue` refuses it, that way fails> --message "Reply with exactly: done Bq"` (`codex queue --help`: "Queue a message for an existing session"); then exit. For each way, B's prompt event fires at enqueue when its UserPromptSubmit `observedAtUtc` is earlier than the `observedAtUtc` of the PostToolUse line for A's `sleep 90` (the PostToolUse line whose command is `sleep 90`) (B was sent while that command ran, so no model step could have taken B in yet), and only when processed otherwise; a B whose prompt event fired at enqueue but that runs as its own later turn (a Stop line carrying B's `turn_id` after A's Stop) also counts as only when processed (queued-message arrival rule, host binding table); a way passes only when its B produced exactly one UserPromptSubmit line (a second line with another `turn_id` would mint a second turn), whose `turn_id` differs from A's and from every other B's, because codex's nonce is `turn_id` (host binding table) and a shared `turn_id` would merge B into A's turn (when a way fires only when processed, P0.2 also records whether the transcript at the payload's `transcript_path` records the enqueue, with its observed payload, for the operator). The codex row fails or is unavailable when run 1 records no UserPromptSubmit line or a payload without `session_id` or `turn_id`, when a way's B, the `codex queue` one included, produces no UserPromptSubmit line, more than one, or one that shares a `turn_id`, or when run 2 cannot be performed; a failed or unavailable codex row, or a result of only when processed for any way, stops P0 and the operator is shown the receipt, because every HV reviewer must persist its own turn through the Codex plugin (host binding table, codex), so every codex arrival path ends either verified or decided by the operator. Afterwards P0.2 copies `observed.jsonl` and run 1's stdout, redacted by the receipt rule of the hostile validation protocol, to `docs/receipts/p0/codex-hook-observer-<utc>.jsonl` and `docs/receipts/p0/codex-hook-probe-exec-<utc>.jsonl`, stops the private app server, signs the probe home out (`env CODEX_HOME=<home> codex logout`), deletes `/tmp/qbrain-p02-codex-<utc>/` (the directory it created), and confirms that `~/.codex/config.toml` still has the recorded SHA-256 and that no file under `~/.codex` contains the probe directory's path (otherwise it stops and shows the operator what changed), so the probe leaves no Codex setting, credential, or path-bearing record in the operator's Codex home; the UserPromptSubmit lines of `codex-hook-observer-<utc>.jsonl` are the recorded codex event payloads that A3's per-host mint cases, A4's mid-turn case, TEST-MCP-PLUGIN-013-AC001, and A9's codex hook cases replay, and that receipt is codex's feature-matrix receipt (P0.8, A9); these runs spawn no subagent, so codex's subagent marker is not observed, and P0.8 records it as unverified in codex's feature-matrix row, the limitation FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC001 provides for; (d) grok: whether `GROK_SESSION_ID` is set in shell commands, and the same enqueue-versus-processing check for UserPromptSubmit; (e) grok-bot: the Grok Bot cloud runtime's hook payload fields, session environment for shell commands, and queued-message behavior; (f) copilot: two real deliveries of one prompt from two registered hooks and whether their `timestamp` values match (evidence only, D11), two identical prompts sent back to back and one sent while a turn runs with whether their `timestamp` values differ, and whether one message ever reaches the hook twice (a host that gives separate messages equal fields or redelivers in normal use is recorded as a D7 limitation, since its sessions fail closed at the first collision), and the same enqueue-versus-processing check for UserPromptSubmit; (g) cline (classic): whether the installed Cline build runs native hooks, where hook scripts are installed, the UserPromptSubmit payload fields, whether a queued message fires the hook at arrival, the same equal-field timestamp and redelivery probe as copilot, the payload's `workspaceRoots` field (one root is supported, a task with several roots is `turn_logging_unsupported`; architecture section 3.1, "Host details"), and whether the hook's `context_modification` output (the required-memories block, FR-MCP-MEMORY-007) reaches the model (architecture section 3.2, host limits); (h) cline-v2: whether every user message starts exactly one run (cline-v2 is supported only then, D6 otherwise; architecture section 3.1), whether a queued message starts its own run at arrival, and if not, whether a message-queue event exists; (i) opencode: whether `chat.message` fires when a message is created while the session is busy, or only when it is processed; (j) every host: whether its plugin API can exclude conversation history from one model request, by replacing the model's message list (opencode `experimental.chat.messages.transform`) or by starting a fresh host invocation whose only input the plugin supplies, recorded with its receipt as the host's Stateless capability (a host without one treats `Stateless` as `Pilot`, RunStatelessInvocation), on cline-v2 including whether the model request its `beforeModel` hook returns replaces the model's messages (architecture section 3.2); and whether its prompt hook can refuse a prompt, and whether the host runs the prompt anyway after a hook timeout, crash, or `plugin_core_unavailable`, recorded as the host's refusal capability (a host that cannot refuse runs `Pilot` and `Stateless` as `Off` with that limitation in its feature-matrix row; architecture section 3.1, resolutions of round 5); (k) every host: whether a host-local command that never reaches the model (for example `/clear`, `/compact`) produces an arrival event (it is then a system event and is never minted; architecture section 3.1), and whether its Stop or stop-equivalent hook can hold the run open (classic cline has none; cline-v2 `afterRun` and opencode `session.idle` fire after the model has stopped, which the architecture states as inference and this probe confirms or refutes; feature-matrix row, architecture section 3.2, host limits), and its prompt-context limit (architecture section 3.1, "Storage budget and sizes"). For (d)-(i), the enqueue-versus-processing check records the event's time against the running turn's progress and whether the message then runs inside the running turn or as its own later turn, read from the order of the host's stop or stop-equivalent events (the `turn_id` criterion of (c) is codex's alone). For every host other than the claude-code/claude-cowork bridge and codex (for codex that result stops P0 for the operator), an enqueue-versus-processing result of "only when processed" (which includes a prompt event at enqueue for a message the host runs as its own later turn, queued-message arrival rule) selects D6 `turn_logging_unsupported` with zero mints and writes in this plan, with or without a recorded enqueue event; when one is recorded, P0.2 records that event's observed payload, and P0.8 files a traced follow-up TODO proposing the host's adapter (payload, nonce, correlation, lifecycle, replay, tests) for operator approval; until that adapter is specified, approved, and delivered, the host stays `turn_logging_unsupported` in this plan and its `<Host>P02NotPassedTurnLoggingUnsupportedZeroMintsZeroWrites` case applies (architecture section 3.2, P0.2 gating rule) (operator D5 requires minting at enqueue; FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC006), and each host's adoption tests (A8, A9) include its recorded case. Each check selects between the outcomes the table already defines; no new design is made, and any host limitation it confirms is shown to the operator.

## Requirements catalog

The new and updated requirements are divided into problem domains (operator, 2026-10-13, verbatim: "Dividing the new reqs into problem domains would be best split."; clarified answer "Catalog and work by domain (Recommended)"). Each domain names the bounded contexts of the architecture document section 8.3 that deliver it and holds its own functional, technical, and testing requirements, AC ownership lines, mapping rows, updated existing records, and test churn. The rules below apply to every domain. The domains, in catalog order, with the new FR, TR, and TEST records each defines:

- Turn arrival and binding: 4 FR, 3 TR, 3 TEST.
- Turn persistence, decision lists, and closing: 4 FR, 5 TR, 5 TEST (TR-SUPPORT-CORE-016 and TEST-SUPPORT-024 among them).
- Request class and the completion gate: 4 FR, 4 TR, 4 TEST.
- Turn reviews, scores, and thresholds: 7 FR, 7 TR, 7 TEST.
- Plugin core runners and host adoption: 2 FR, 4 TR, 4 TEST.
- Context packages and token budget: 2 FR, 2 TR, 2 TEST (outline; P0 completes the agent-context catalog).
- Responses, grading, trust, and response lifecycle: 3 FR, 3 TR, 3 TEST (outline).
- Relationships, retrieval, and GraphRAG projection: 2 FR, 2 TR, 2 TEST (outline).
- Agent-context surfaces, mode, and stateless invocation: 2 FR, 2 TR, 2 TEST (outline).
- Shared platform: dispatch, workspace resolution, storage, and errors: 0 FR, 1 TR, 1 TEST (TR-MCP-SHAREDPLATFORM-001 and TEST-MCP-SHAREDPLATFORM-001).
- Build tooling: 0 FR, 6 TR (TR-MCP-NUKE-003, -004, and -011 to -014, under FR-MCP-BUILD-001), no TEST.

Totals: 30 FR, 39 TR, 33 TEST (Phases A and B: 21 FR, 25 TR with TR-MCP-NUKE-003 and -004, 23 TEST; Phase C: 9 FR, 9 TR, 9 TEST; the prerequisite Nuke TRs TR-MCP-NUKE-011 to -014; the shared-platform TR and TEST). Each domain's "Updated existing requirements" subsection is the complete list of the live records it changes, and P0.9's store-versus-catalog diff counts the updated records from those lists.

AC ids. The store holds AC ids in only a few families (the TR-MCP-NUKE TRs and TR-MCP-SYNC-001 as `AC-NNN`, FR-MCP-BUILD-001 as `ac-1`, and the ACKRECOVERY, RUNAS, SERVICEUPDATE, QBRAIN-003, QBTOOLS-000, and USECASE-017/019 records); no AC of any family this plan updates carries an id, and the `<REQID>-AC###` form exists nowhere in the store (2026-10-12 export, recon req-ab.md T4; the requirements renderer prints AC text and evidence, never an id, `src/QBrainAi.Services/Requirements/RequirementsDocumentRenderer.cs:189-203` at `a8d3934b`). P0 therefore assigns the ids when it records each record: every AC of a new record and every AC of an updated record gets `<REQID>-AC###` (three digits, 1-based in store order; for example `FR-MCP-SESSIONLIFE-001-AC004` is the fourth AC of FR-MCP-SESSIONLIFE-001), and P0.4 resends an updated record's full AC list with those ids, its existing ACs first in store order and its new ACs after them; a record whose ACs already carry ids keeps them and continues in its own form (the TR-MCP-NUKE family's `AC-NNN`, which the Nuke TRs of this plan follow). In this catalog a label `ac-N` or `acN` on an updated record names its Nth AC in store order and is recorded as `<REQID>-AC00N`; "inline AC (N)" names the Nth prose AC of the record's statement, which P0 records as a structured AC with that id (a record that today carries only inline prose ACs gets structured ACs); `AC-NNN` after a new record's id is shorthand for `<REQID>-ACNNN`; and in the allocation lists the shorthand `SESSIONTURN-004-001` means `FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC001`. The bracket after each AC names the slice (A1 to A9, S1 to S11) or scope entry (the Phase C entries, SharedPlatform, and the prerequisites P0.1a and P0.1c) whose RED test first covers it; "+" lists later slices or entries that extend coverage on another surface. Each AC is verified by the TEST listed under its TR, except a TR AC that names Build.Tests cases as its evidence (every AC of the Build tooling domain's TR-MCP-NUKE TRs, and only those; the clear-session skill parity evidence lives in PluginIntegration (G3) and each plugin repo's Pester `Skills.Tests.ps1` (G6), never in Build.Tests, architecture 3.1 "Test placement"): those cases, run in the Build.Tests infrastructure step (`dotnet test build/Build.Tests`, the one exception to the Nuke rule, architecture 8.4; BDPv4 contract), verify it, and no FR AC or TEST record in this catalog cites a Build.Tests case. Nuke behavior lives only in TR-MCP-NUKE TRs (here TR-MCP-NUKE-003, -004, and -011 to -014, and the Build.Tests paths of TR-MCP-NUKE-002 and -006 to -010), which are implementation detail of the build-tooling FR FR-MCP-BUILD-001 "Tooling for repeatable builds" and have no TEST record (operator, 2026-10-10: "NUKE is only accountable through TR."; the decision "Separate Nuke-only TRs"; and "FR: Project will provide tooling for repeatable builds." / "Now, the TR are implementation detail of the FR."); TR-MCP-SYNC-001, the one Nuke TR the store maps to an application FR with a TEST record, is retired and moved under FR-MCP-BUILD-001 (Build tooling domain); every TR that maps to an application FR keeps only application ACs. FR-MCP-BUILD-001 is tested by running `./build.ps1 -WhatIf` (TR-MCP-NUKE-005, TODO PLAN-BUILDWHATIF-001, outside this plan). Each new TEST record carries exactly the three ACs of the rule in the next paragraph.

Each TEST id mirrors its TR. P0 creates each record with the title and statement below (exactly: the title is the row text after its id up to ` Tests:`, without its final period; in the statement, <AC list> is the row's Validates and Also validates lists with ranges such as AC001..AC004 expanded, and <test classes> is its Tests: list); the statement is "Validates <AC list> with <test classes>; every listed new or changed AC has a RED receipt before implementation and a green receipt at the gate of the work package that delivers it, and every listed baseline-green AC has a green receipt." Every record carries the same three ACs, with its own id prefix: `<TESTID>-AC001` every listed new or changed FR and TR AC has at least one named test that failed (RED) before implementation and passes after, and every listed baseline-green AC has a named test that passes; `<TESTID>-AC002` the mocks-green run and its negative check are recorded on the Phase 3 unit-tests TODO (architecture 8.2); `<TESTID>-AC003` the listed suites run with zero failures and zero skips in the gate of the work package that delivers them. (TEST-SUPPORT-024 carries the same three texts as its AC001..AC003, under the AC id rule above.) Record text and TEST ids: the strict traceability run (`./build.ps1 ValidateTraceability --strict-tr-and-test-coverage`) reads every hyphen-joined `TEST-` token on every line of `Testing-Requirements.md` as a TEST id and reports one the matrix lacks (`build/TraceabilityValidator.cs:15-16,41-49,147`, `build/Build.ValidateTraceability.cs:57-74` at `a8d3934b`), and that file prints each TEST record's statement and AC text. So the text P0 writes into a TEST record names a TODO whose id starts with `TEST-` by its role, never by its id (`TEST-MERGEDPLAN-001` is written "the Phase 3 unit-tests TODO"), and names the AC of a TEST record as the TEST id, a space, and the AC suffix (`TEST-MCP-SESSIONLOG-003 AC001`), never hyphen-joined; this plan's own text, which the run does not read, keeps the ids. Span and satisfaction: a TEST record belongs to the slices or scope entries in its TR's bracket, every slice or entry labelled in parentheses in its row (for example `(S5)`), and every slice or entry, earlier or later, whose task line names one of its cases or test suites. So TEST-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008 spans S1, S8, and S9 (S9 names its TypeScript-host cases; the real-gate cases belong to the TEST-MCP-SESSIONCLASS records), TEST-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-009 spans S8, S9, and S11 (whose task names `SessionLogReviewDocsContractTests`), and TEST-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001 spans S1, S3, S4, S5, and S6. Each work package's gate runs the cases its scope entries own plus those of every earlier entry in the span, and the record's three ACs are set satisfied only when the TODO that delivers the last scope entry of its span passes its HV. Every case a row names is owned by a slice of that span. Where an AC's bracket names several surfaces, each TEST covers the surfaces its own span implements, and the other surfaces are covered by the TESTs of the slices that implement them (a row says so explicitly where it hands one off, as in "via TEST-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004").

AC ownership (round-3 finding R3-F15): every new or changed FR or TR AC is owned by exactly one TEST record. When more than one row below names an AC, its owner is the one this list states (rule: a TR AC is owned by the TEST that mirrors its TR; an FR AC by the first TEST in its FR's mapping row that names it; a new AC of an updated TR that no TEST record mirrors, for example TR-MCP-CQRS-001, by the TEST its domain's AC-ownership list names), and every other naming row contributes its named cases to the owner instead of validating the AC: P0 writes the owner's statement with the AC in its <AC list> and every contributed case, with the contributing row's test classes, in its <test classes>, and writes each contributing record's statement without that AC, naming it only as "contributes <cases> to <AC> (owner <TEST>)". P0.9 verifies in the store that every new or changed AC appears in exactly one TEST record's AC list.

Each row is passed literally as one `workflow.requirements.createMapping { frId, trIds, testIds }` call; every id is the full canonical id, and P0.4 checks each id with `getTr`/`getTest` before the call. A row may name TESTs only (`createMapping` needs at least one TR or TEST id, `src/QBrainAi.Repl.Core/RequirementsWorkflow.cs:476-479` at `a8d3934b`). An edge a domain removes is listed under its "Mapping removals" and removed by one `workflow.requirements.deleteMapping { frId, trId }` or `{ frId, testId }` call, which rewrites that FR's mapping without the one id and keeps every other edge (`:545-600`).

Each row is one more `workflow.requirements.createMapping { frId, trIds, testIds }` call in P0.4. `createMapping` merges with the existing edges (`src/QBrainAi.Repl.Core/RequirementsWorkflow.cs:518-523` reads the current mapping and writes the union), so every pre-existing edge is preserved. Each TEST listed here names, in its P0 statement ("Also validates updated ..."), the updated-requirement ACs shown in brackets together with the named test case; those ACs follow the same RED-before-code and green-at-gate receipts as every other changed AC. Every new AC on an updated TR or TEST record is likewise named in exactly one TEST statement with a named case, except a TR AC whose evidence is a Build.Tests case, which names that case itself (every AC of the Build tooling domain's TR-MCP-NUKE TRs), and an AC split by side is owned once, with each side's case named (FR-MCP-SESSIONLIFE-003-AC008 is owned by TEST-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004 for the server side, and TEST-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005 contributes the plugin side's case; AC-ownership list), and every changed existing AC is allocated to the slice whose RED test first covers it (TDD AC allocation); an updated-record AC without both is a P0 HV FAIL.

### Domain: Turn arrival and binding

Bounded contexts (architecture 8.3): SessionTurnLogging (MintSessionTurnCommand and its minter, and the delegating OpenSessionLogCommand, SetTurnTitleCommand, and SetSessionTitleCommand), PluginCorePowerShell (`plugins/core/lib-ps`: the prompt hook, the arrival bridge's own script entry, verb binding, the section 3.5.6 functions in `plugins/core/lib-ps/repl-invoke.ps1`, the core Pester suites, and `tests/QBrainAi.PluginIntegration.Tests`), and HostPluginAdoption (A2's validator and installer in the Claude plugin repo, and the host-repo cases of `#### Test churn`). Calls into SharedComponents (SerializableWrite, IdempotentCreate, ReadableKeyAllocator with strategy `RandomHex`, `ClassifiedErrorException`) go through their Phase 2 seams. Use cases: UC-1 (architecture 7.2). Operator decisions this domain cites (architecture 3.1): D1, D2, D5, D6, D9, D11, "Revive, keep MCP session (Recommended)" (2026-10-12), "Mint isolation", "Compaction and resume", and "Plugin function home".

#### Functional requirements

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001 One turn per user request, with a server-issued request token. Every user message that reaches a host arrival mechanism (prompt hook, arrival bridge, or host-native hook adapter; D5, D6) maps to exactly one session-log turn whose `requestId` the server mints at ingress (operator D1).
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC001 The plugin opens every turn through the server mint operation (`POST /qbrainai/sessionlog/{agent}/{sessionId}/mint` with an `ingressNonce`, or its client and REPL equivalents, called only by hooks and adapters); the server mints `req-<server UTC yyyyMMddTHHmmssZ>-<8 lowercase hex random>`, opens the turn `in_progress`, and returns the token; the plugin never invents a requestId. The plugin's `workflow.sessionlog.beginTurn` verb is retired: no tool descriptor names it, and a call is refused with `mint_requires_host_ingress` before any server call (architecture 4.1; `repl-invoke.ps1:2161,3047` at `a8d3934b`; `BeginTurnVerbRetiredRefusedMintRequiresHostIngress`). A minted requestId is unique within the workspace, enforced by the database for minted turns across all sessions; a violation of that requestId index makes the server generate a new requestId (at most 5 retries after the original attempt, 6 attempts in all, then 500 `mint_collision_exhausted`) and never merges into another turn, while a violation of the nonce index means the same event and returns the existing requestId (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC002) (`MinterCollisionReturnsDistinctId`, `MintCollisionConcurrentCrossSessionDistinctIds` on SQLite, PostgreSQL, and SQL Server). The mint is a single-turn write under turn-level isolation (architecture 3.1, "Mint isolation", and 4.2, MintSessionTurnCommand): it reads its session row only to locate the parent and writes only the one turn it inserts (architecture 3.5.2, isolation scope), so a mint never answers a conflict because of a write to another turn of the same session (`MintConcurrentWithOtherTurnWriteSameSessionBothCommitNoConflictAnswer`); the six-attempt bound covers request-id collisions only, and a transient conflict (SQL Server 1205, PostgreSQL 40001 and 40P01, SQLite 5 and 6) uses SerializableWrite's four attempts and then answers 409 `conflict` with `retryable: true` and saves nothing (`MintTransientConflictFourAttempts409ConflictRetryableNothingSaved`, three providers), which the plugin handles as an outage (AC008). [A1 server, A3 plugin]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC002 Repeating the mint with the same agent, MCP session id, and `ingressNonce` (network retry, two hook processes started for one event, or failsafe replay after an outage) returns the same requestId and opens no second turn (server behavior on every host; on copilot and classic cline the hook applies the equal-field rule to a `merged` result of a new invocation, so it fails the host session closed instead of binding, while a stored mint retry or failsafe drain binds normally). A same-nonce mint for a turn that is already terminal returns `merged` with that `storedStatus`, leaves the status unchanged, and the hook neither makes it the write target nor adds it to the open list or the stop ledger (`MintSameNonceTerminalTurnMergedNotReopened` server side; `PromptHookSameNonceTerminalTurnMergedNotTargetNotOpenNotLedger` plugin side). A prompt hook killed at any point after its write-ahead mint record (before the mint call, after the server committed the mint, or before its delivery replace) ends at the next hook, verb, or SessionStart drain with exactly one server turn for that message, delivered once: the drain's mint with the same nonce returns the stored turn, which enters the open list and the ledger and becomes the write target only when no later request was delivered in that host session, and a merged terminal turn is never delivered (architecture 3.1 and 4.4, DrainFailsafe "Delivery once"; what-if W-4; `PromptHookKilledAfterServerMintBeforeDeliveryNextDrainOneTurnDeliveredOnce`). [A1 server, A3 plugin, A4 plugin write target, open list, ledger, and killed-hook delivery]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC003 Two user messages with identical prompt text (an intentional repeat, at any interval; on copilot and classic cline the equal-field rule governs a repeat that also shares the timestamp) are two events, derive two nonces, and receive two requestIds and two turns; two independent hook processes receiving one event (a doubly registered hook) derive one nonce from the host binding table and receive one requestId on every host whose nonce is a stable per-message id (codex, grok, grok-bot, cline-v2, opencode; cline-v2 is supported only when P0.2 shows one run per user message, otherwise D6, architecture 3.1; on bridge hosts the single bridge per host session mints, TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001 AC-006); on copilot and classic cline a second registration is refused before any mint (D11 and the classic-cline single-registration check), so the positive two-process case does not apply there. On copilot and classic cline, whose nonce hashes event fields with no per-message id, two messages whose timestamps differ mint two turns, a second new invocation with equal session, timestamp, and prompt cannot be told apart from a separate message, so it mints nothing and the host session fails closed under D6 (`indistinguishable_arrival`) rather than merging or guessing, and a retry or failsafe replay of one stored mint reuses its nonce (`CopilotSamePromptDifferentTimestampTwoMints`, `CopilotEqualFieldsSecondInvocationFailsClosedNoSecondMint`, `CopilotMintRetryAndFailsafeReplayReuseNonceNoFailClose`, `ClineSamePromptDifferentTimestampTwoMints`, `ClineEqualFieldsSecondInvocationFailsClosedNoSecondMint`, `ClineMintRetryAndFailsafeReplayReuseNonceNoFailClose`). [A1 server, A3 plugin, A8 TypeScript hosts +A4 +A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC004 Distinct prompts open distinct turns, and on every prompt-hook and adapter host whose P0.2 row passed, each prompt form of AC009 that the host delivers to its hook (including a prompt of at least 1 MiB, non-ASCII, CRLF, and image-only) mints exactly one turn (what-if W-11; `PromptHookPromptFormsOneMintEachPerHost`; on the TypeScript hosts through the PowerShell core, `TsHostPromptFormsOneMintEach`). [A3 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC005 A message delivered while another turn is in progress (queued or mid-turn) gets its own turn, minted by the host's arrival mechanism (arrival bridge, prompt hook, or host-native hook adapter); the running turn is not changed. [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC006 The Claude hook validator reports `duplicate` when an MCP hook script is registered by both `~/.claude/settings.json` and the enabled plugin, repairs it with a backup, and `-CheckOnly` never mutates settings; when the backup cannot be written, no settings file changes and the validator reports the failure (architecture 4.4, ValidateHookRegistration; what-if W-25; `RepairBackupWriteFailsNoSettingsChangeReportsFailure`). [A2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC007 The hook installer adds no bridge hook for an event the enabled plugin already covers. [A2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC008 When the server is unreachable at ingress, the hook persists a failsafe mint record carrying its nonce and opens a provisional local turn `pending-<nonce>`; on failsafe drain the server mints idempotently from the nonce and the plugin rebinds the provisional turn and every write bound to it to the minted requestId, in original order. Either id resolves to the same turn; a crash at any point during the rebind loses no write and creates no second turn; a committed pending-to-minted alias stays discoverable and is reapplied until every consumer of it (including an audit outbox) durably acknowledges it, and duplicate delivery or application is harmless (C2). With two or more consumers, an alias acknowledged by one stays discoverable to the others; replay to a consumer stops only after that consumer durably acknowledges, and stops entirely once all have. No server turn ever has a `pending-` requestId: a write captured under a pending id and sent after the alias is sent under the minted id, and the writes of one turn reach the server in their original order even when a close of that turn was made while earlier writes waited in the failsafe. A mint that the server or REPL does not support is recognized only by the trusted mint adapter (A3, in the PowerShell core) for the exact mint operation, from either an authenticated, bound mint-endpoint response with HTTP 404 and no detail code, or the structured `unknown_method` (GenericClientPassthrough) or `method_not_found` (ReplCommandDispatcher) result for the expected mint verb, as on a server older than A1; it is never inferred from prose, malformed detail metadata, arbitrary 404s, or unrelated operations, keeps the original structured cause, and is classified as a transport outage by that adapter only (never by the generic C9 classifier or the audit outbox in-call retry loop) and handled like one (failsafe retained, turn stays `pending-<nonce>`, retried at a later drain). `Resolve-MintOutcome` (architecture 3.5.6, in `plugins/core/lib-ps/repl-invoke.ps1`) is the one classifier of a mint answer for the prompt hook, the arrival bridge, and the drain: a transport outage, a retryable 503 (`backend_unavailable`, or a busy store's `persistence_error` with `retryable: true`), 409 `conflict` with `retryable: true`, the scoped mint 404 or `unknown_method` above, and `session_not_open` while the session open for the record's envelope is not confirmed are outages (`MintRetryable409Or503OutagePendingIdFailsafeRecordKept`); a mint refused for a final reason (`ingress_nonce_invalid`, `validation_error` including an unresolved workspace's 400 with `details.field` `workspacePath`, `mint_collision_exhausted`, or `session_not_open` after a confirmed session open) reports `turn-open-failed` with the server code and invents no requestId; on the prompt hook its write-ahead mint record counts that answer as its first drain attempt (`drainAttempts` 1), the drain mints it again under the attempt budget of 5, each final answer counting one more, and then moves it to the quarantine subdirectory with its `.reason.txt` (architecture 3.1, "Failsafe drain"; `PromptHookFinalMintRefusalRecordKeptDrainAttemptsOne`; the budget and quarantine cases are the persistence domain's drain cases); on the bridge it becomes an `open-failed` queue entry that the bridge never mints again (AC010); and the stop gate warns naming the prompt while the record is queued or quarantined (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC005). A verb whose bound id is a `pending-<nonce>` id, or whose host session's failsafe holds drainable records, first runs the drain; when the pending id still has no journaled alias, or a record of the bound turn stays queued, the write is captured in the failsafe behind them (`queued`) and is never sent under the pending id, on the submit, dialog, and title routes (architecture 4.4, PersistTurnWriteWithReceipt; what-if W-10; `PendingTargetServerReachableVerbDrainsMintFirstNeverSendsPendingIdAllRoutes`). On drain the plugin first replays an idempotent session open for the record's envelope `mcpSessionId` (an outage that spanned SessionStart left that session unopened on the server), then the mint; a `session_not_open` refusal is final only after that session open is confirmed (`OutageSpanningSessionStartDrainOpensSessionThenMintsNotFinal`). The pending-to-minted lookup entry is permanent for the life of the plugin cache: retiring an alias's consumer-delivery obligation after the last required acknowledgement never removes it, so a reader holding the pending id resolves the minted turn after every acknowledgement and after a restart. After the delivery obligation is retired, `getAlias` still returns the full agreed record with the exact final acknowledgement set, and a repeated acknowledgement is an idempotent no-op. [A3 +A5 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC009 Arrival bridge (D5): on claude-code, and on claude-cowork when P0.2 shows Cowork runs plugin hooks and passes the same bridge probes as claude-code (otherwise FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC004 applies: Cowork reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and the feature matrix records the limitation), the arrival bridge is the only minter: every user message is minted from its transcript `enqueue` record (nonce `q-` from that record) before the model processes it, in state `queued`, and the prompt hook neither mints nor claims (in `Pilot` or `Stateless` it applies pending records through `Sync-PluginArrivalLog` and takes the bridge-minted requestId, still minting nothing; architecture 4.4, ApplyArrivalBridgeRecord, specified under FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-009), so no hook-to-bridge correlation exists to fail (Claude Code's `queue-operation` records carry no parent `promptId`; the plan never correlates across record kinds without a shared key). One message yields exactly one mint for every prompt form P0.2 captures (plain, slash command, attachment or image, image-only, multi-line CRLF, non-ASCII, at least 1 MiB), and two messages with equal text yield two mints. A pid file naming a dead or foreign process does not prevent a bridge start; a host session ended by the idle rule (`ended.yaml` with `reason: idle`) is live again at its next mint or keyed verb from that host session key, which removes the marker in the same atomic replace under `turn-open.lock` before acting; a keyed verb that revives a session with no delivered request is then refused with `request_unknown` (no write target); a `reason: session_end` marker is revived only by a SessionStart for the same host session key (Claude Code resume and `codex exec resume` reuse the key), which removes it in one atomic replace under `turn-open.lock`, as the idle revival does, and continues in the host session's recorded MCP session, because `Invoke-SessionOpen` never replaces a recorded `mcpSessionId`; a keyed verb never revives it (operator, 2026-10-12, verbatim answer "Revive, keep MCP session (Recommended)"; architecture 3.1 and 4.4, StartHostSession and EndHostSession) (`IdleEndedKeyedVerbRevivesAtomicallyNoTargetRequestUnknown`, `IdleEndedRevivalCrashRestartConsistent`, `SessionEndMarkerResumeSessionStartRevivesKeepsMcpSessionId`, `SessionEndMarkerKeyedVerbDoesNotRevive`); a resumed or forked transcript mints nothing for queue records written before that host session's SessionStart; a transcript shorter than `appliedThroughOffset` is reported with `arrival_bridge_format_unrecognized`, changes no queue entry, records `unsupportedSince` with that reason after a delivered request (unsupported-session rule), and the host session reports `turn_logging_unsupported`, with writes for its already open turns going to the plugin failsafe, until the bridge restarts on a valid transcript (fail closed, like an unknown record shape); the bridge restarts from SessionStart after `/clear` and resume; it stops when `ended.yaml` with `reason: session_end` is written for its key, while an idle end does not stop it, so a message enqueued after an idle end is still minted at enqueue (`IdleEndedThenMidTurnMessageMintedAtEnqueue`); and a bridge whose running version differs from the active plugin cache is treated as a stale pid, stopped, and restarted from the active cache with `appliedThroughOffset` kept (architecture 3.1 and 4.4, RunArrivalBridge; FR-MCP-TRIAGEPLUGIN-001; `BridgeRunningOtherCacheVersionRestartedFromActiveCacheOffsetKept`). If P0.2 shows a user prompt that produces no `enqueue` record, a `dequeue` that does not take the head, or a request whose `dequeue` line (or `remove` with `absorbed_mid_turn`) is not in the transcript before that request's first tool call starts, the host is `turn_logging_unsupported` (D6) (architecture 3.1, "Late `dequeue` on claude-code", which settles what-if W-9; `ClaudeCodeP02NotPassedTurnLoggingUnsupportedZeroMintsZeroWrites`; the real prompt-form fixture case runs only when those probes pass); when they pass, a verb started at a request's first tool call binds to that request on its P0.2-recorded transcript (`BridgeDequeueRecordedBeforeFirstToolCallVerbBindsToDequeuedRequest`). [A3 +A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC010 Queue lifecycle: a `queued` request becomes `delivered` (and the host session's write target) at `dequeue` (the head of the queue) or at `remove` with reason `absorbed_mid_turn`, and becomes `withdrawn` at a `popAll` or a `remove` with any other or no reason; minting never moves the write target; a withdrawn request stays `in_progress` on the server until the model closes it (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC009). A record that cannot be attributed (equal content with no shared key, or a record that matches nothing: a `remove` matching zero or two or more queued entries, or a `popAll` in which, for some content, the line count differs from the number of queued entries of any kind with that content, or that leaves a queued entry unpopped; a `dequeue` with no queue head is an unrecognized format and fails closed with `arrival_bridge_format_unrecognized`; architecture 4.4, ApplyArrivalBridgeRecord; what-if W-7) is never guessed: the host session fails closed under D6 (the bridge stops before the record, `unsupportedSince` is recorded, every session-log verb in that host session reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and writes nothing to the server until it ends, writes for its already open turns go to the plugin failsafe and drain only after that host session ends or a SessionStart clears its cause (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001; architecture 4.4, DrainFailsafe), and its open turns are listed with their pairs at the next SessionStart); a `popAll` whose line counts equal the queued entries of every content it carries and that pops every queued entry withdraws every queued request entry in progress and drops every other queued entry. Every `enqueue` takes a queue position: system events as unminted `kind: system` entries, so a `dequeue` whose head is a system entry delivers nothing and the user request behind it stays `queued` until its own delivery (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC001). A user `enqueue` whose mint is refused for a final reason becomes a `kind: open-failed` entry with the server code and no requestId, and a request entry whose mint returned `merged` with a terminal `storedStatus` keeps that status; each keeps its queue position, so a later `dequeue`, `remove`, or `popAll` that takes it delivers and withdraws nothing, and the bridge never mints an `open-failed` entry again (the Stop gate's warning naming its prompt is FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC005) (architecture 4.1 request lifecycle and 4.4 ApplyEnqueueRecord, ApplyDequeueRecord, ApplyRemoveRecord; what-if W-8; `BridgeRemoveMatchingNoEntryFailsClosed`, `BridgePopAllExtraLineOrUnpoppedEntryFailsClosed`, `BridgeDequeueEmptyQueueFormatUnrecognizedFailsClosed`, `BridgeFinalMintRefusalOpenFailedKeepsPositionDequeueDeliversNothing`, `BridgeMergedTerminalAtMintKeepsPositionDequeueDeliversNothing`). [A3 +A4]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002 Background and system events never open turns.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC001 Prompts that are `<task-notification>`, `<local-command-*>`, `<agent-message ...>` subagent hand-backs, or only system-reminder blocks, payloads marking a subagent (Claude Code `agent_id`, Grok `subagentType`), deliveries with `MCP_SUBAGENT_ID`/`GROK_SUBAGENT_ID` set, and a host-local command that never reaches the model (for example `/clear`, `/compact`) when P0.2 shows it produces an arrival event (architecture 3.1; `HostLocalCommandWithArrivalEventSystemEventNoMint`, run on the P0.2-recorded payload where one exists) open no turn, call no mint, leave turn state unchanged, and report `system-event-skipped`; classification uses these structural markers only, never the topic or wording of the prompt. On every supported host a prompt event produced by a subagent or a child session (for example an opencode `task` child session's `chat.message`) opens no turn; P0.2 records each host's subagent marker, and a host without one records the limitation in the feature matrix. On bridge hosts a system-event `enqueue` still occupies a queue position (`kind: system`), so its later `dequeue` or removal never delivers or withdraws a user request (`BridgeSystemEnqueueAheadOfUserSystemDequeueUserStaysQueued`, `BridgeSystemEnqueueRemovedNoTurn`, `BridgeSystemAndUserInterleavedCrashReplayZeroWrongTurnWrites`; the `MCP_SUBAGENT_ID`/`GROK_SUBAGENT_ID` deliveries `SystemEventsSubagentEnvMarkersNoMint`; the opencode child session `OpenCodeChildSessionChatMessageNoTurn`). [A3 +A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC002 Slash-command (`<command-name>`) prompts that reach the model and `<user_query>` prompts open turns (a host-local command is a system event under AC001), and so does any user prompt that happens to contain a hostile-review or delegation brief (for example the brief that launches a Codex hostile reviewer, which needs its own reviewer turn), because the prompt hook classifies a background agent only by the host's subagent markers, never by prompt text; this replaces FR-MCP-TRIAGEPLUGIN-001's text-detected background-brief reuse (`Test-PluginPromptIsBackgroundAgent` and `Get-PluginRootTurnIsolationDecision`, `plugins/core/lib-ps/plugin-hook.ps1:693,725` at `a8d3934b`; architecture 5.4 and 7.8) and opens the new turn without changing any other turn (`HostileReviewBriefPromptOpensOwnTurnAgentIdPayloadDoesNot`). [A3]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002-AC003 Hooks of subagents or parallel sessions sharing the workspace cache never change this session's turn state. [A4]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003 A new prompt never cancels an in-progress turn.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC001 Opening a turn never persists `canceled` for any other turn. [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC002 The prior in-progress turn stays `in_progress` locally and on the server and remains addressable by requestId. [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC003 Only the model closes turns; no hook calls complete or fail. [A7]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003-AC004 The stop ledger lists every request delivered since the last passing Stop, plus every earlier entry whose close the server has not confirmed; a request that is minted but still `queued` is not in it until it is delivered (the single enrollment rule of FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001). Only a server-confirmed close leaves the ledger (A5 outcome mapping, `CloseVerbOnlyServerConfirmedCloseLeavesOpenListAndLedger`), and the Stop gate evaluates that ledger plus one separate scan of `withdrawn` queue entries still `in_progress` (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC009, TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-006 AC-005), so a request withdrawn before delivery is blocked as `turn_withdrawn_unclosed` without ever entering the ledger (A7, `StopGateLedgerListsDeliveredSinceLastPassPlusUnconfirmedCloses`, `StopGateWithdrawnBeforeDeliveryEmptyLedgerBlocksWithdrawnUnclosedFailByPairNextStopPasses`). [A4 +A5 +A7]

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007 Independent turn edits.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC001 Each turn has its own state file and lock; each host session has one state file `sessions/<hostSessionKey>/turn-state.yaml` holding its write target (the request most recently delivered to the model; D9 refuses unqualified writes while several delivered requests are open), its open delivered requests, its queue (minted requests with `queued`/`delivered`/`withdrawn` state), `appliedThroughOffset`, and the host session's own `mcpSessionId`, which StartHostSession (on the TypeScript hosts, which have no SessionStart event, the first ingress event for that host session key, A8) records only when none is recorded for the key, always replaced atomically (temp file, flush, rename) under `turn-open.lock` through `Update-TurnState` (architecture 3.5.6); `current-turn.yaml` remains only as a read-only legacy status file that no verb uses for targeting. The arrival bridge, the mint, and every verb read the MCP session id only from their own host session's turn-state; the workspace-level `session-state.yaml` keeps develop's trust and header fields and is never read for targeting, and the rotated-sessionId rebind (`repl-invoke.ps1:1954-1965` at `a8d3934b`, TR-MCP-PLUGIN-012) is removed, because `Assert-ReplCurrentTurnFresh` keeps only its marker-drift check (architecture 4.1), so a second host session's SessionStart never retargets the first (`TwoHostSessionsOwnMcpSessionIdsSecondSessionStartNeverRetargetsFirst`, TypeScript hosts `TsHostTwoHostSessionsOwnMcpSessionIdsSecondNeverRetargetsFirst`). A later SessionStart, a claude-code PostCompact run (which runs StartHostSession for the same key), or a resumed key for a host session key that already has a recorded `mcpSessionId` keeps it: no new MCP session is opened for that key, its open turns stay bound and close in their original MCP session, no `unsupported_session` record of that still-live host session is drained, and a PostCompact run lists nothing and emits no `additionalContext` (FR-MCP-115, TR-MCP-PLUGIN-009, and TEST-MCP-155 kept) (architecture 3.1, "Compaction and resume", which replaces TR-MCP-PLUGIN-012's rotation rebind; what-if W-3; `PostCompactSameHostSessionKeyKeepsMcpSessionIdOpenTurnClosesInOriginalSession`, `PostCompactUnsupportedSessionCapturedRecordsNotDrained`). StartHostSession first runs develop's trust bootstrap (`Invoke-FullBootstrap` with the marker snapshot, `plugins/core/lib-ps/plugin-hook.ps1:286-308` at `a8d3934b`) and agent runtime header capture (`:314-326`, TR-MCP-PLUGIN-HEADER-001) unchanged: an unverified marker records `MCP_UNTRUSTED` in the workspace `session-state.yaml` as today and ends the process before the session open, with no server call; a new `mcpSessionId` comes from develop's `New-PluginSessionId` (`:47-56`), the `MCP_SESSION_ID` override when it is set, else `<Agent>-<yyyyMMddTHHmmssZ>-<suffix>`, and host sessions that share an override share one MCP session while each keeps its own `turn-state.yaml` (architecture 3.1, "Host session start state"; C5; `SessionStartUnverifiedMarkerRecordsUntrustedNoSessionOpenNoServerCall`, `SessionStartNewMcpSessionIdFromNewPluginSessionIdOverrideHonored`). A prompt hook whose host session has no recorded `mcpSessionId` (no SessionStart ran for its key) first runs StartHostSession for that key from its own payload (the session key, and on claude-code and claude-cowork the `transcript_path` that starts the bridge; on a host without a SessionStart event, the first-ingress writer), so an unreachable server is then the mint's outage (`turn-opened-degraded`) and the drain opens the session before it mints; this replaces develop's no-session recovery (`Invoke-PluginNoSessionRecovery`, `plugin-hook.ps1:541`, status `no-session` at `:778`; architecture 3.1, "Prompt without a recorded session"; `PromptHookNoRecordedSessionRunsStartHostSessionForKeyThenMints`). The plugin's `openSession` verb resolves its host session as a verb does and opens that host session's recorded `mcpSessionId` through `Invoke-SessionOpen` (OpenSessionLogCommand), writing no `session-state.yaml` (architecture 4.1; `repl-invoke.ps1:243-293` at `a8d3934b` writes only that file today; `OpenSessionVerbOpensOwnHostSessionMcpSessionIdNoSessionStateWrite`). [A4 +A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC002 Every verb resolves exactly one target turn, in this precedence: explicit `requestId` override, else the invocation's bound request, which is refused with `request_ambiguous` while two or more delivered requests are open in the host session (D9); it mutates only that turn; an explicit requestId resolves to exactly one immutable owning MCP session within the validated workspace and agent, and naming a turn of an earlier host session requires its original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair, used only by recovery and review backfill (C5); an unknown explicit requestId is refused with `request_unknown`, and a terminal one with `turn_immutable` (except the review and class writes allowed on terminal turns, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC004). A verb whose host session has no delivered request (no write target) is refused with `request_unknown` naming the host session key and writes nothing (`BindingNoWriteTargetRequestUnknownNamesHostSessionKey`). With no delivered request open and a write target set, an unqualified verb targets that write target even when it is closed, and every verb other than `recordReview` and a requestClass-only update is refused with `turn_immutable` (architecture 4.4, BindVerbToTurn, "Closed write target"; what-if X-7; `BindingClosedWriteTargetNoOpenRequestOtherVerbsTurnImmutable`; the acceptance of the review and class-only writes is FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC004 and FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC003, from S8). A `request_ambiguous` refusal lists each open delivered request's requestId, delivery time, source, and title. [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC003 Concurrent edits to two turns from separate processes lose nothing and cross-contaminate nothing, and concurrent edits to one turn from separate processes (for example parallel `appendActions` and `appendDecisions`, serialized locally by that turn's own lock `turns/<requestId>.lock`, architecture 3.4) lose no item locally or on the server (what-if W-14; `ConcurrentEditsSameTurnTwoProcessesNoLostItem`). [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC004 Failsafe records and fingerprints are namespaced by requestId; replay never targets another turn. A failsafe record without a captured binding (written before the upgrade) is never bound to a turn by any later binding: on every drain trigger (SessionStart, the next hook or verb, and the explicit `workflow.failsafe.drain` verb) a record that names no request (it relied on `current-turn.yaml`) moves to the quarantine subdirectory with its `.reason.txt`, and a record whose parameters name its session and request replays its method and parameters onto that turn as today (architecture 3.1, "Failsafe drain", and 4.4, DrainOneFailsafeRecord; what-if W-27); a turn in progress in the legacy `current-turn.yaml` at upgrade is listed with its pair by the first SessionStart, warned by the Stop gate, and stays addressable by explicit requestId (`FailsafeLegacyNoRequestQuarantinedOnEveryDrainTrigger`, `FailsafeLegacyNamingSessionAndRequestReplaysOwnTurnOnly`, `LegacyCurrentTurnInProgressListedAtFirstSessionStartAddressableByRequestId` in A4; the Stop warning `StopGateLegacyCurrentTurnInProgressWarned` in A7). [A4 +A7]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC005 Opening, closing, or canceling one turn never changes another turn locally or on the server. [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC006 Host-context binding (operator D2): at the start of each verb invocation the plugin resolves the invoking host session's key by the host's locked method (see the host binding table) and binds the invocation to that host session's write target; the binding is written into the invocation's persist and failsafe records and never re-resolved, so a prompt minted after the invocation started never retargets it. On hosts with an arrival bridge, the invocation records the transcript length when it starts and then applies, under `turn-open.lock`, exactly the transcript records that end at or before that length (records appended after it started are left for later invocations), so the binding is the invocation-start snapshot D2 requires and reflects every delivery recorded before the invocation began. A hook payload (prompt, SessionStart, PostCompact, or SessionEnd) without the host row's session key refuses with `host_session_unknown` and writes nothing; the live-session rule applies only to verbs (architecture 3.1, which settles what-if Q-12, and 4.4, OpenTurnFromPromptHook and StartHostSession; `HookPayloadWithoutHostSessionKeyRefusedNothingWritten`; the Stop hook's same refusal is FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005's, architecture 4.4 EvaluateStopGate). The `Invoke-McpPlugin.ps1 -Command CompleteTurn` entry (`plugins/core/lib-ps/Invoke-McpPlugin.ps1:344-351`, `plugins/core/lib-ps/final-response.ps1:27` at `a8d3934b`) is the `completeTurn` verb: process BindVerbToTurn binds it from its invocation context like every verb, and it carries no evidence of its own, so its close passes `Test-ModelEvidence` and `Get-CloseRemedy` like any close (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC002) (architecture 3.1, "`final-response.ps1`"; `FinalResponseCompleteTurnEntryBoundLikeVerbNoEvidenceRefusedWithRemedy`). [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC007 Interleaving: with R1 delivered and R2 enqueued mid-turn in the same host session, a verb started after R2's mint but before R2's delivery (`absorbed_mid_turn` or `dequeue`) writes to R1; a verb started after R2's delivery without `requestId` is refused with `request_ambiguous` while R1 and R2 are both open (D9); with explicit ids an R1, R2, R1 sequence of writes lands on R1, R2, R1 respectively; once R1 is closed an unqualified verb writes to R2; a withdrawn R2 never becomes a write target; R1 stays in progress throughout. When the verb cannot read its host session key, the live-session rule applies: with exactly one live host session of its agent it binds there with warning `host_session_inferred`; with none or several it is refused (`host_session_unknown`, `host_session_ambiguous`) unless it carries the trusted (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair of C5 that the server verifies for this workspace and agent; a `requestId` alone is refused the same way; with two concurrent host sessions in one workspace and no keys, zero writes land on the wrong turn. After a prompt's open fails (`turn-open-contended`, `turn-open-failed`, `nonce_source_missing`), unqualified verbs in that host session are refused with `request_unknown` until a later delivery succeeds, so that prompt's work never lands on the earlier turn (`BindingAfterFailedOpenUnqualifiedVerbRequestUnknown`). A turn the server reports terminal, whichever surface closed it, stops counting as an open delivered request at the next verb or Stop, and a request leaves the open list only when the server confirms its close (a queued, refused, or failed close leaves it open; A5 outcome mapping `CloseVerbOnlyServerConfirmedCloseLeavesOpenListAndLedger`, A7 Stop `StopGateTurnClosedElsewhereDroppedFromOpenList`). When the write target is closed while an earlier delivered request is still open, an unqualified verb other than `recordReview` and a requestClass-only update is refused with `turn_immutable` naming the open request (architecture 4.4, BindVerbToTurn, "Closed write target"; `BindingWriteTargetClosedEarlierStillOpenTurnImmutable`). [A4 +A5 +A7]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC008 Session changes in place never misattribute: after Claude Code `/clear`, a shell verb binds to the new session's write target (its `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` is updated), a long-lived process resolves per call by the live-session rule (the cleared session is ended by SessionEnd reason `clear`), and after resume the resumed session's key is used; a resumed key whose `ended.yaml` says `session_end` is revived by its SessionStart and keeps its recorded MCP session (AC001, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC009; `SessionEndMarkerResumeSessionStartRevivesKeepsMcpSessionId`). [A4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC009 Every record family the plugins write for a turn (queue entry, alias, failsafe record, stop-ledger entry inside `turn-state.yaml`, persisted or queued event) carries the root-id envelope `{ workspacePath, agent, mcpSessionId, hostSessionKey, ingressNonce, requestId }` (`requestId` may be the pending alias until rebind), while a session-scoped record that belongs to no turn (a `kind: system` queue entry, `unsupportedSince`) carries the session-level envelope `{ workspacePath, agent, mcpSessionId, hostSessionKey }` plus its own key (`enqueueOffset`, `causeOffset`, on a prompt-hook host `causeNonce`, or, for a cause with neither, its `reason` and `detectedAtUtc`), never an invented nonce or requestId, so each record is attributable on its own without consulting another record (operator, 2026-10-09: "The root id of whatever something is a child of or depends on, should always be carried."; design principle: "Always have a traceable relationship when coordinating two disparate entities."); each family is read back after write and after replay with the envelope intact (`RootIdsEveryRecordFamilyPresentAfterWriteAndReplay`, including the alias). [A3 +A4]

#### Technical requirements

- TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001 Turn-open lock, ingress mint client, system-event classifier (lib-ps). AC-001 `turn-open.lock` serializes opens across processes in one workspace cache; it is taken only through `Update-TurnState` (architecture 3.5.6), and the prompt hook takes it once and never waits: a contended open reports `turn-open-contended`, writes the same failsafe diagnostic as `nonce_source_missing` naming the prompt, invents no nonce, and mints nothing (architecture 3.1 and 4.4, OpenTurnFromPromptHook; what-if W-5; `ContendedTurnOpenLockReportsTurnOpenContendedNoMint`, `ContendedTurnOpenLockDiagnosticWrittenNamingPrompt`; the Stop warning is FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC005's); a process that dies while holding the lock never blocks a later acquirer, and its change is either fully applied or absent, as the atomic replace guarantees (what-if W-6; `TurnOpenLockHolderKilledNextAcquirerProceedsStateConsistent`); AC-002 `Get-PluginIngressNonce -Host <host> -Payload <payload>` derives the nonce from the event exactly as the host binding table states for each hook host (a pure function: equal payloads give equal nonces), and when a payload lacks the field its host row names, the hook does not invent a nonce: it reports `turn-open-failed` with reason `nonce_source_missing` and writes a failsafe diagnostic, which the stop gate shows as warning `nonce_source_missing`; the nonce is written into the failsafe mint record before the first mint call, and the host binding table equal-field rule is applied under `turn-open.lock` before that write (`indistinguishable_arrival`); the arrival bridge keeps no write-ahead mint record, because the transcript is its durable source (architecture 4.4, ApplyArrivalBridgeRecord; what-if X-3); AC-003 classifier returns true exactly for the FR-002 event list; AC-004 hook-originated verbs carry `MCP_PLUGIN_HOOK_INVOCATION=1`; AC-005 `Invoke-PluginIngressMint` calls the server mint with the nonce through the REPL verb `workflow.sessionlog.mintTurn`, statelessly with explicit agent, sessionId, and nonce, and classifies the answer through `Resolve-MintOutcome` (architecture 3.5.6: the one classifier for the prompt hook, the arrival bridge, and the drain, in `plugins/core/lib-ps/repl-invoke.ps1`, the dot-sourceable library that `plugin-hook.ps1` dot-sources, architecture 3.1, "Plugin function home"); the verb's response carries the FR-MCP-REPL-006 deprecation marker that the dispatcher puts on every `workflow.*` response (`src/QBrainAi.Repl.Core/ReplCommandDispatcher.cs:212-222,330` at `a8d3934b`) and that `Resolve-MintOutcome` ignores when it maps the result (what-if W-26; `MintTurnReplDeprecationMarkerIgnoredByCore`); the mint client records the returned requestId (a prompt-hook mint is delivered at once, so it also becomes the host session's write target; a bridge mint stays `queued` until delivery), and on outage opens `pending-<nonce>` and rebinds it on drain through the C2 alias journal `sessions/<key>/aliases.yaml` (`Add-McpAlias`, architecture 3.5.6: its `lookup` entry, carrying the root-id envelope of FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-007-AC009, and its `delivery` entry committed in one atomic replace before any queued record is rewritten; readers resolve either id through `Get-McpAlias`; replay reapplies a journaled alias without minting; the consumer functions are TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 AC-007's); AC-006 `Start-PluginArrivalBridge` (claude-code, claude-cowork) is launched by SessionStart (process RunArrivalBridge) as a detached `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive` process of the bridge's own script entry in `plugins/core/lib-ps`, which dot-sources `repl-invoke.ps1` for the section 3.5.6 functions (architecture 3.1, "Plugin function home"), with the workspace root as working directory, writes `sessions/<key>/arrival-bridge.pid`, tails `transcript_path` from `appliedThroughOffset`, applies each `queue-operation` record by the D5 lifecycle in the host binding table (every `enqueue` takes a queue position, system events as unminted `kind: system` entries; mint at a user `enqueue`, deliver at `dequeue` and `absorbed_mid_turn`, withdraw at other `remove` and `popAll`) to `turn-state.yaml` in one atomic replace that also advances `appliedThroughOffset`, skipping records that start below it, exits when `ended.yaml` with `reason: session_end` is written for its key (an idle end does not stop it; what-if X-6), a second start for the same key exits immediately, and a pid naming a dead or foreign process, or a bridge running another plugin cache version, is replaced by a bridge started from the active cache with `appliedThroughOffset` kept (architecture 4.4, RunArrivalBridge; `BridgeStalePidRestarts`, `BridgeRunningOtherCacheVersionRestartedFromActiveCacheOffsetKept`, `IdleEndedThenMidTurnMessageMintedAtEnqueue`); `Sync-PluginArrivalLog` (also called by the Stop hook and SessionStart, TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003 AC-007) records the transcript length when a session-log verb starts and, after the verb takes `turn-open.lock`, applies only complete records that end at or before that length before the verb binds (records appended later are left for later invocations; `BindingDequeueAppendedAfterStartBeforeLockBindsToEarlier`), and on claude-code and claude-cowork the prompt hook neither mints nor claims. A4 extends AC-006 with the verb-side halves of the bridge cases, and A5 extends AC-005 with the rebind of real queued writes through the persist path. [A3 +A4 +A5]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-002 Hook validator and installer (Claude plugin repo). AC-001 `Test-PluginEnabled` reads the `enabledPlugins` map and `known_marketplaces.json`; AC-002 `Get-DuplicateHookCommands` lists event:script duplicates; AC-003 validator JSON adds `duplicate`, `repaired`, `duplicateHooks`, `pluginEnabled`, `pluginKey`; AC-004 repair writes a backup and keeps non-MCP hooks; when the backup cannot be written, no settings file changes and the validator reports the failure (architecture 4.4, ValidateHookRegistration). [A2]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-003 Per-turn store, pointer, target resolution, stop ledger (lib-ps). AC-001 `turns/<requestId>.yaml` plus `.lock`; the turn file holds the turn's `lastBuildStatus`, which code-verify (`Invoke-CodeVerify`, `plugins/core/lib-ps/plugin-hook.ps1:1199` at `a8d3934b`, which today writes it into `current-turn.yaml` at `:1209,1262`) writes into the write target's file under that turn's lock, finding the host session from the hook payload's key as a verb does (a payload without the key writes nothing), and which the Stop gate's failed-build block reads (architecture 3.4 and 4.1; `CodeVerifyWritesLastBuildStatusToWriteTargetTurnFile`); AC-002 `Resolve-ReplTargetTurn` contract (process BindVerbToTurn, nested activity ResolveVerbTarget: precedence explicit requestId, else invocation binding; an unqualified verb is refused with `request_ambiguous` while two or more delivered requests are open, D9; an unknown explicit id is refused with `request_unknown`; with one or no delivered request open the target is the write target even when it is closed, where every verb but `recordReview` and a requestClass-only update is refused with `turn_immutable`); AC-003 `Invoke-ReplSupersedeCurrentTurnIfInProgress` removed; AC-004 stop-ledger contract (`ledger` inside `turn-state.yaml`, written in the same atomic replace as the delivery it records); AC-005 per-turn edits never overwrite another turn's title (absorbs the intent of retired TR-MCP-REPL-019); AC-006 `Get-PluginHostSessionKey` implements the host binding table (invocation-context key, else, for verbs only, the live-session rule with `host_session_inferred`, `host_session_unknown`, `host_session_ambiguous`; a hook payload without the host row's key refuses and writes nothing), and `New-ReplInvocationBinding` captures `{ hostSessionKey, keySource, ingressNonce, requestId, boundAtUtc }` (C4; `ingressNonce` is the bound turn's nonce) once per verb invocation into its persist and failsafe records; AC-007 `sessions/<hostSessionKey>/turn-state.yaml` is written only by the arrival bridge, or a verb, the Stop hook, or SessionStart applying pending transcript records through `Sync-PluginArrivalLog` up to the transcript length at its own start (the Stop payload carries `transcript_path`; `StopGateBridgeDeadAppliesPendingRecordsBlocksDeliveredUnclosed`) (mint, delivery, withdrawal, `appliedThroughOffset`), the prompt hook on hosts without a bridge (mint and delivery), the close verbs (removing a request from the open delivered list only when the server confirms its close), the failsafe drain (replacing `pending-<nonce>` with the minted id in the write target and open list), the Stop gate (clearing ledger entries of confirmed-closed turns on a passing Stop, and recording `stop_gate_overridden_by_host` with the open turn ids when the host ends the session anyway), the first verb or hook in a host session that observes a failing copilot or classic-cline single-registration check (recording `unsupportedSince` with reason `duplicate_prompt_hook`), the first ingress event of a host session on a host without a SessionStart event (recording that host session's own `mcpSessionId` only when none is recorded, A8), and SessionStart (process StartHostSession, which a claude-code PostCompact run also runs, as does a prompt hook whose host session has no recorded `mcpSessionId`: recording a new `mcpSessionId` from `New-PluginSessionId` only when none is recorded for the key, after develop's trust bootstrap and header capture, and clearing `unsupportedSince` where its cause's own rule allows), each as one atomic replace under `turn-open.lock` through `Update-TurnState`, the one writer of the file (architecture 3.5.6); and `sessions/<hostSessionKey>/ended.yaml` (`{ reason: session_end | idle, endedAtUtc }` with the session-level envelope) only by the session-end hook (process EndHostSession, which also stops the bridge), the 12-hour idle rule, the revival of an idle-ended session by its next mint or keyed verb, and the revival of a `session_end`-ended key by a SessionStart for that key (each removal atomic under `turn-open.lock`; architecture 3.1, "Resumed host sessions"). From Phase C, `agentContextMode` and its two Mode cache writers (architecture 3.4) are specified in FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-009's catalog. The arrival bridge, the mint, and every verb read the MCP session id only from their own host session's turn-state; the workspace-level `session-state.yaml` keeps develop's trust and header fields and is never read for targeting (architecture 3.1, "Host session start state"), and the rotated-sessionId rebind (`repl-invoke.ps1:1954-1965`, TR-MCP-PLUGIN-012) is removed (`TwoHostSessionsOwnMcpSessionIdsSecondSessionStartNeverRetargetsFirst`, `PostCompactSameHostSessionKeyKeepsMcpSessionIdOpenTurnClosesInOriginalSession`, `SessionEndMarkerResumeSessionStartRevivesKeepsMcpSessionId`). A5 extends AC-007 with the close verbs' server-confirmed removal, and A7 with the Stop gate's writes. [A4 +A5 +A7]
