### Domain: Turn reviews, scores, and thresholds

Bounded contexts (architecture 8.3): TurnReviews leads (the section 3.5.4 components `ScoreLexeme` and `ScoreLexemeJsonConverter` in `src/QBrainAi.Client/Scoring/`, `ScoreVerdict` in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Scoring/`, and `ReviewDefaults` in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Reviews/`; the review validator, mapper, and threshold provider; the `SessionLogSanitizer` review paths through `SecretRedactor`; RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand and ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand, record and handler in two files each, in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Reviews/Commands/`, namespace `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Reviews.Commands`; policies ReviewValidation and HvVerdictMapping). StorageSchema delivers S3 (the review columns, the reason table, the migration, the backfill, the database defaults); SessionTurnLogging delivers the S4 review wiring inside `SessionLogService.cs` and `TransactionGatedSessionLogService.cs` and `TurnReadProjection`; ReplAndAgents delivers S6 and S7; PluginCorePowerShell delivers S8; the Phase 2 shared files (`DESIGN-MERGEDPLAN-001`) hold the S1 review DTOs in `src/QBrainAi.Client/Models/SessionLog/` and the S5 controller, MCP tool, and client surfaces with their error mapping; Documentation delivers S11. Use cases: UC-6 to UC-9 (architecture 7.7 to 7.10).

#### Functional requirements

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001 Turn review records.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC001 Each turn exposes `agentReview` and `hostileReview` with fields `status`, `otherNotes`, `correctness`, `completeness`, `correctnessThreshold`, `completenessThreshold`, `reasons`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`, `requestJsonlPath`, `responseJsonlPath`. [S2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC002 The server creates both records at turn creation, on every insert path, with `ReviewDefaults` for the turn's class (architecture 3.5.4): `agentReview` `Unstarted`; `hostileReview` `Unnecessary` when the turn is created with class `Chore`, otherwise `Unstarted`. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC003 A review write replaces the whole record (latest wins, reasons not merged); an identical write is a no-op. A no-op is a write whose resulting stored record (derived status and stamps included) equals the current one. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC004 An omitted review keeps the stored record (the `Merge` modes); a PUT turn omitting it and a review section DELETE (REST, client `ClearTurnReviewAsync`, and MCP `sessionlog_clear_section` with a review section or alias, each dispatching ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand) reset it to the default status. The default status is the creation default for the turn's current class (`ReviewDefaults`): `hostileReview` `Unnecessary` when the class is Chore, otherwise `Unstarted`; `agentReview` `Unstarted`. On a `completed` or `failed` turn a review section DELETE is refused with 400 `review_section_reset_on_terminal_turn` and changes nothing; a turn that is `in_progress`, `canceled`, `cancelled`, or `closed` is reset (architecture 5.2, ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand). [S4 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC005 Every read surface returns both records through `TurnReadProjection` (architecture 3.5.3): review text stored as written and redacted by `SecretRedactor`, scores as their exact decimal lexemes, unset fields `null` (REST, MCP, client, REPL, federation snapshot, transaction restore). [S4 +S5 +S6]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC006 Existing rows, soft-deleted rows included, are backfilled with `agentReview=Unstarted` and `hostileReview=Unstarted`, and a revived row reads them; a turn the prior binary inserts during a binary-only rollback reads both as `Unstarted` after the roll-forward, which runs no second backfill (the database default, architecture 5.1 and 8.5). [S3]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002 Review writes on any turn status.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC001 Section PUT `agentReview`/`hostileReview` works on in-progress, completed, failed, canceled, and legacy turns and changes no other field or status, on every write surface: REST PUT, client `RecordTurnReviewAsync`, MCP `sessionlog_record_review`, and MCP `sessionlog_replace_section` with a review section or alias, each dispatching RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand with the same policies and codes (a non-review section keeps today's direct service call, `src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp/McpStdio/FwhMcpTools.SessionLog.cs:203-253` at `a8d3934b`). [S4 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002 `recordReview` (MCP tool, client, REPL, plugins) writes only the review; for a turn of an earlier MCP session (review backfill, including the D10 HV verdict) the plugin surfaces accept only that turn's original (MCP `sessionId`, `requestId`) pair, verified against the workspace and agent and captured once, and refuse a `requestId` alone or a pair from another conversation with `request_unknown` (C5). Non-plugin surfaces (MCP tool, REST, client, REPL) answer a pair that does not exist for the named agent in the workspace with 404 `not_found` (`TurnLocator`; a review never creates a turn), which the plugin reports as `request_unknown` (architecture 5.5, ServeReviewWrite). The REPL verb `workflow.sessionlog.recordReview` dispatches statelessly with explicit agent, sessionId, and requestId and carries the FR-MCP-REPL-006 deprecation marker, which the PowerShell core ignores when it maps the result. [S5 +S6 +S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC003 Other mutations of terminal turns stay refused by the REPL and plugins. requestClass-only updates and review writes are the exception: the REPL, plugins, and server accept them on terminal turns (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-004-AC004), and an unqualified `recordReview` or requestClass-only update binds to the host session's write target even when that turn is closed (architecture 5.5, recordReview sequence). [S6 +S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC004 Item DELETE on a review section is refused with 400 on REST and refused the same way on MCP `sessionlog_delete_item` (`FwhMcpTools.SessionLog.cs:256-279` at `a8d3934b`); the existing delete-item handler refuses it and it never reaches a review unit. [S4 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC005 A review write that keeps conflicting with a concurrent write to the same turn returns 409 `conflict` with `retryable: true` once SerializableWrite's default bound (four attempts) is spent, and saves nothing. A review keeps its no-failsafe rule (an older review must never overwrite a newer one), so the plugin `recordReview` verb reports that answer, a transport outage, 503 `backend_unavailable`, and a busy store's 503 `persistence_error` with `retryable: true` each with `persisted: false`, the answer's code, and `retryable: true`, exit 1, never `queued`, and the model records the review again for the same turn after the drain (architecture 3.1 "Retryable answers", 5.2 RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand, UC-6). [S4 +S5 +S8]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003 Per-status field validation.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC001 `Unnecessary`/`Unstarted` carry no data fields. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC002 `Running` requires `reviewer` and forbids scores. `Running` may carry `reviewedAt` and forbids scores, `reasons`, `otherNotes`, and receipt paths; a write with status omitted and fewer than two scores is rejected with `review_field_required_for_status` naming the missing score; `agentReview` `Pass`/`Fail` may carry receipt paths. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC003 `Pass`/`Fail` require `correctness`, `completeness`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`; `hostileReview` `Pass`/`Fail` also require both jsonl paths. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC004 `Other` requires `otherNotes` with at least one non-whitespace character (rejected otherwise with `review_other_notes_required`); scores, `reasons`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`, and receipt paths are optional. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC005 `otherNotes` with text is rejected unless status is `Other`. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC006 Scores lie in [0,1]; `reviewer` <= 256 chars; paths <= 2048 chars; `reviewedAt` needs an explicit offset and is stored UTC; reason items are non-empty. Each violation returns exactly one code from the wire-contract error list, identical on REST, MCP, client, REPL (the REPL classifier carries the server's `ClassifiedError` through at every status, architecture 3.5.2), and the PowerShell plugin core (golden contract). The field-to-code map is: `reviewer` over 256 characters or a receipt path over 2048 characters gives `review_field_too_long` naming the field and limit; `reviewedAt` without an explicit offset or not a valid ISO-8601 instant gives `review_timestamp_invalid`; an empty or whitespace-only reason item gives `review_reason_blank`; each is asserted with the same code and HTTP 400 by the golden contract in C# and Pester (`GoldenContractFieldToCodeMapAllSurfaces`: C# in S1, REST/MCP/client in S5, REPL in S6, Pester in S8). When one write has several violations, every surface returns the first in one order: the score lexeme codes at deserialization, then the `hvVerdict` checks, then the order of policy ReviewValidation (architecture 5.2 RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand and 5.3 ReviewValidation), and the golden contract carries multi-violation cases. [S1 +S5 +S6 +S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC007 Validation runs before any mutation on every surface, including a review record carried in a turn body (submit, upsert, PATCH, PUT, complete, fail), which policy ReviewValidation checks with the same codes before the class and the completion gate are evaluated; score lexemes are validated at deserialization, before any lookup, so an invalid score on an unknown turn is 400, not 404. [S4 +S5 +S6]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004 Lossless decimal scores.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC001 `0.1234567890123456789` and `0.1234567890123456789012345678` round-trip unchanged through every hop: client JSON [S2], SQLite/PostgreSQL/SQL Server storage [S3], REST and MCP [S5], REPL YAML quoted/unquoted and JsonElement [S6], PowerShell plugin YAML block/flow and JSON [S8], the TypeScript-host adapters, which never parse a score [S9].
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC002 A JSON number or numeric string is accepted when its raw text matches `^[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?$` (no sign, no exponent) with a value in [0,1] (`ScoreLexeme`, architecture 3.5.4); a normalized number (trailing fractional zeros stripped) is returned. [S1 +S2]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC003 Validation runs on the raw lexeme before any numeric conversion, through the one rule `ScoreLexeme` (`ScoreLexemeJsonConverter` at deserialization on the server and client, the REPL normalizer, and the PowerShell core's `Protect-ReplLosslessScalars`, kept in step by the golden contract), so nothing is ever rounded: more than 28 fractional digits is rejected with `review_score_precision` (e.g. `0.12345678901234567890123456789`, which `JsonElement.TryGetDecimal` would round), exponent or signed forms with `review_score_format`, and values outside [0,1] with `review_score_out_of_range`; each case is tested as a JSON number, a JSON string, an unquoted YAML scalar, and a quoted YAML scalar. [S1 +S5 +S6 +S8]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005 Hostile validation receipt mapping. Scope (architecture 5.4, process ConvertHvReceiptToReview): the HostileReview subsystem (FR-MCP-HOSTILEREVIEW-001 to -006, its durable queue, its worker pinned to effort `xhigh`, and its AGREE, DISAGREE, or UNKNOWN verdicts) is separate from a turn's `hostileReview`; its results are not HV receipts for this record and never pass through this mapping. UC-7's live model and effort rule (gpt-6-astra, max) is an HV-protocol rule the coordinator checks from the reviewer's receipts before recording any verdict, not a plugin check.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC001 `accuracy/100` maps to `correctness` and `completeness/100` to `completeness` exactly. [S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC002 The plugins send the receipt's verdict as `hvVerdict` (AGREE or DISAGREE) with the mapped scores and no status, and the server maps it atomically at that write (policy HvVerdictMapping, architecture 5.3) with the hostile thresholds in force at that write, per instance through `McpInstanceResolver`. Before any mapping the server refuses an `hvVerdict` other than AGREE or DISAGREE with 400 `review_invalid_status` and `details.field` `hvVerdict`, an `hvVerdict` write missing either score with 400 `review_field_required_for_status` naming the missing score, and an `hvVerdict` sent with an explicit `status` with 400 `validation_error` and `details.field` `status`. The effective verdict is AGREE only when no reason starts with `FAIL:` or `UNKNOWN:`; otherwise the write maps as DISAGREE, the rule the plugin applies when it sets `hvVerdict` (AC003), so an MCP, client, or REPL caller and the plugin store the same status for the same fields (golden contract, C# and Pester). AGREE stores the derived Pass or Fail; DISAGREE stores Fail when the scores derive Fail, otherwise `Other` with a nonempty `otherNotes` chosen deterministically: the failed claims (FAIL entries of `reasons`) when present, otherwise the UNKNOWN claims, otherwise the verdict-level reason `DISAGREE with no listed failed or unknown claims` plus the response receipt path (`HvVerdictMappingDisagreeUnknownOnlyPassScoresOtherNamesUnknownClaims`, `HvVerdictMappingDisagreeNoListedClaimsPassScoresOtherVerdictLevelReason`). No client reads thresholds, and a mapped receipt never returns `review_status_conflict` (`HvVerdictMappingInstanceOverride095Scores097And099AgreeStoresPassDisagreeStoresOther`). `hvVerdict` travels unchanged through the client and the MCP tool (`HvVerdictClientAndMcpToolForwardedToServerMapping`, S5) and the REPL validator, schema, and flat-arg allow-list (`ReplRecordReviewHvVerdictValidatorSchemaFlatArgsForwarded`, S6). `hvReceiptPath` is owned by the plugin verbs (the PowerShell core, S8, for every host): the plugin reads and maps the receipt and sends the mapped fields with `hvVerdict`, and never forwards `hvReceiptPath` to the REPL, MCP tool, client, or server. `hvVerdict` on an agent review is refused with 400 `validation_error` and `details.field` `hvVerdict` (`SessionLogTurnReviewSectionTests.AgentReviewWithHvVerdictValidationError`, S4). [S4 +S5 +S6 +S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005-AC003 `reasons` (each FAIL and UNKNOWN claim of the receipt, prefixed `FAIL:` or `UNKNOWN:`), the plugin sends `hvVerdict` AGREE only when the receipt's OverallVerdict is AGREE and every claim is PASS, otherwise DISAGREE (`HvReceiptAgreeWithFailOrUnknownClaimSentAsDisagree` in S8), `reviewedAt`, `reviewer` come from the receipt, and a DISAGREE receipt with empty FAIL and UNKNOWN lists and no `reasons` is valid input; the Codex HV receipt (the reviewer's JSON receipt and the `--json` response jsonl) is accepted as input. A receipt is refused by the plugin before any write with one code from the wire-contract error list naming the field: a verdict that is not AGREE or DISAGREE with `review_invalid_status` (field `hvVerdict`), a missing accuracy or completeness with `review_field_required_for_status` (field `correctness` or `completeness`), an accuracy or completeness outside 0..100 with `review_score_out_of_range`, and a jsonl path that does not exist with `review_receipt_paths_required` (field `requestJsonlPath` or `responseJsonlPath`) (`HvReceiptInvalidRefusedBeforeWriteExactCodes` and `HvReceiptDisagreeUnknownOnlyEmptyReasonsOtherNotesNamesUnknownClaims`, `HvReceiptDisagreeNoListedClaimsEmptyReasonsOtherNotesVerdictLevelReason` in S8); the server stores receipt paths as text without checking that they exist. [S8]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006 Review status lifecycle.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC001 Status accepts the six values case-insensitively and returns PascalCase. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC002 `hostileReview` flips `Unstarted` -> `Unnecessary` when the class becomes Chore, and back when the class changes away while still `Unnecessary` (`ReviewDefaults`). The flip and its reverse apply only to a turn that was nonterminal (`TurnStatus`, architecture 3.5.3) before the write, so a write that sets `Chore` and completes the turn in one call still flips the hostile record; a class change on a turn already terminal never changes either review record (policy RequestClassification, architecture 5.3). [S1 rule, S4 service]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC003 Records past `Unstarted`/`Unnecessary` are never changed automatically, also under concurrency: a Chore flip and a client review write to the same turn commit wholly before or after each other (SerializableWrite with turn-level isolation, architecture 5.2), so a flip that read `Unstarted` never overwrites a concurrent `Running` write, on SQLite, PostgreSQL, and SQL Server. [S1, S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC004 Clients cannot write `Unnecessary` or `Unstarted`. The only client path that yields those statuses is a section DELETE or a PUT turn omitting the review, which resets to the default (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC004), or a requestClass change (AC002); a review body naming either from an agent surface is rejected with `review_unnecessary_not_client_writable` (the Import and Replication origins store a supplied record as received, FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-003-AC002). [S1 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006-AC005 Clients may write `Running`, `Pass`, `Fail`, `Other` from any state, including reruns. [S1]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007 Configurable pass thresholds.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC001 The thresholds `ReviewThresholds:{Agent|Hostile}:{Correctness|Completeness}` under `SessionLog` are declared under the canonical root `QBrainAi:` in `appsettings.yaml` (which already holds `QBrainAi:` with `SessionLog:` under it, `src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp/appsettings.yaml:46,332` at `a8d3934b`) and read as `Mcp:SessionLog:ReviewThresholds:*` after `CanonicalSectionProjectionProvider` projects both roots onto `Mcp:` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/Options/CanonicalSectionProjectionProvider.cs:7-8,29` at `a8d3934b`: within one layer `QBrainAi:` wins, and a legacy `Mcp:` key keeps working through 1.x as FR-MCP-QBRAIN-004 allows), with per-instance override through `McpInstanceResolver`; default 0.98 for all four (architecture 5.1). The `ConfigValidator` check under both roots is TR-MCP-NUKE-004, citing TR-MCP-NUKE-008. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC002 Pass iff both scores meet their thresholds (inclusive, exact decimal), derived by `ScoreVerdict` (architecture 3.5.4). [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC003 An omitted status is derived; a supplied `Pass`/`Fail` that contradicts the derivation is rejected with 422 `review_status_conflict` and details. [S1 +S4 +S5]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC004 `Other` is never derived. [S1]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC005 Thresholds in force are stamped on `Pass`/`Fail` records and are read-only. Changing thresholds never re-derives or restamps an existing record; a later write to that record derives and stamps with the thresholds in force at that write. [S4]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC006 Startup rejects thresholds outside [0,1]. [S4]

#### Technical requirements

- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001 Review storage. AC-001 per-kind columns on `SessionLogTurns` (architecture 5.1: `Status` 16 required, `OtherNotes`, `Correctness`, `Completeness`, `CorrectnessThreshold`, `CompletenessThreshold` with `HasPrecision(29,28)`, `Reviewer` 256, `ReviewedAt` UTC, `RequestJsonlPath` and `ResponseJsonlPath` 2048); AC-002 `SessionLogTurnReviewReasons` table of `SessionLogTurnReviewReasonEntity { Id, WorkspaceId, SessionLogTurnId, Agent, SessionId, RequestId, Kind, Ordinal, Value }`, whose `Agent`, `SessionId`, and `RequestId` are its turn's key, copied from the turn in the same transaction, never changed, and in no key or index (architecture 3.1 "Root ids"); its wiki dump policy, `COPY-SANITIZE+HISTORY` listed with the session-log child tables, is FR-MCP-WIKIEXPORT-003's record and case (architecture 3.1 "Review reason dump policy" and 5.1, `WikiDumpReviewReasonsCopySanitizeHistory`, shared-platform domain); AC-003 the review part of the `_AddSessionLogTurnReviewsAndRequestClass` migration for each of the three providers, with the backfill of every existing row, soft-deleted rows included, and the database default `Unstarted` on each review `Status`, so a turn the prior binary inserts during a binary-only rollback reads as its `ReviewDefaults` after the roll-forward, which runs no second backfill; Up, Down, and Up on SQLite, PostgreSQL, and SQL Server leave the schema and the data equal (architecture 5.1 and 8.5); AC-004 graph paths (stamp, detach, include, soft-delete, revive, transaction restore, federation). [S3 +S4]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002 DTOs and serialization. AC-001 the review DTOs `SessionLogTurnReviewKind`, `SessionLogTurnReviewInput`, and `SessionLogTurnReviewDto` are defined once, one type per file, in `src/QBrainAi.Client/Models/SessionLog/` (namespace `QBrainAi.Client.Models.SessionLog`) and the server uses them (no server copy), while `requestClass`, `agentReview`, and `hostileReview` are added to both develop definitions of `UnifiedRequestEntryDto` (`src/QBrainAi.Client/Models/SessionLogModels.cs:108`, `src/QBrainAi.Services/Models/UnifiedSessionLogDto.cs:135` at `a8d3934b`), the one type kept twice, whose server and client parity is checked (architecture 3.5 and 5.1); AC-002 `ScoreLexemeJsonConverter` (namespace `QBrainAi.Client.Scoring`, validating the lexeme at deserialization through `ScoreLexeme`, architecture 3.5.4) and the UTC converter; AC-003 source-gen registrations; AC-004 `TurnReadProjection` (architecture 3.5.3) is the read shape of every review: text through `SecretRedactor`, scores as exact lexemes, unset fields `null`, and its review-text redaction and the TR-MCP-SESSIONLOGSAN-001 decorator's review paths are the same `SecretRedactor` call (the component and its one rule set are TR-MCP-SHAREDPLATFORM-001 AC-006's); AC-005 the search text (`BuildSearchText`, `src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/SessionLogService.cs:1367` at `a8d3934b`) includes reviewer, reasons, and otherNotes and excludes requestClass, scores, thresholds, and paths; the text filter runs over the raw stored turn, so a query for a raw value the sanitizer redacts in a review field still finds its turn, which reads back redacted (FR-MCP-SESSIONLOGSAN-001 ac4 kept), and a reason present only in a replaced record no longer matches (architecture 3.4 Search and 5.2 QuerySessionLogsQuery: the former "a redacted review value matches no turn" clause is removed). [S1 +S2 +S4]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003 Review units, sections, client, MCP tool. AC-001 `agentReview`/`hostileReview` sections (case-insensitive; aliases `agent-review`, `agent_review`, `hostile-review`, `hostile_review`) on `PUT` and `DELETE /qbrainai/sessionlog/{agent}/{sessionId}/{requestId}/sections/{section}` dispatch RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand and ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand, and a known non-review section stays on the existing handlers (`src/QBrainAi.Support.Mcp/Controllers/SessionLogController.cs:576-601,606-628` at `a8d3934b`); AC-002 client `RecordTurnReviewAsync`/`ClearTurnReviewAsync`; AC-003 MCP `sessionlog_record_review(agent, sessionId, requestId, kind, reviewJson, workspacePath)`, which returns `{ success: true, review }`, and its descriptor JSON; MCP `sessionlog_replace_section` and `sessionlog_clear_section` called with a review section or alias dispatch RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand (reading `sectionJson` as the same `SessionLogTurnReviewInput`) and ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand, every non-review section keeps the direct service call (`FwhMcpTools.SessionLog.cs:203-253` at `a8d3934b`), the two tools' descriptions list the two review sections beside the nine they list today (`:203,231`; architecture 3.1 "Host details"), and `sessionlog_delete_item` on a review section is refused like the REST item route (`:256-279`); AC-004 every review refusal surfaces through the one `ClassifiedErrorException` branch of `McpErrorClassifier` (architecture 3.5.2: no per-feature branch): 400 with the review code in `code`, 422 `review_status_conflict` with the thresholds in force and the derived status in `details`, and on MCP `{ code, error, message, retryable, details }`, while an unresolved workspace is the shared 400 `validation_error` with `details.field` `workspacePath` from WorkspaceResolutionBehavior after the dispatch (shared-platform domain; on REST the middleware answers first with its own body); AC-005 RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand and ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand (record and handler in two files each in `src/QBrainAi.Services/Reviews/Commands/`, namespace `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Reviews.Commands`) delegate their save to `ISessionLogService.ReplaceTurnSectionAsync` and `ClearTurnSectionAsync` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/ISessionLogService.cs:108,121` at `a8d3934b`), which run `TurnLocator` and the save in one SerializableWrite with turn-level isolation inside the existing decorator chain (TR-SUPPORT-CORE-014 ac-5), so a review reset and a concurrent close of the same turn never both commit, and when the default bound (four attempts) is exhausted the unit returns 409 `conflict` with `retryable: true` and saves nothing (architecture 5.2). [S4 +S5 +S10]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004 REPL. AC-001 `IReplJsonNumberNormalizer` (process PreserveReviewScoreLexemes, architecture 5.4) holds no lexeme rule: it calls `ScoreLexeme` for `correctness` and `completeness` inside `agentReview`, `hostileReview`, and `recordReview` and forwards each lexeme unchanged (the agent-context grade keys it also covers belong to the agent-context domain); AC-002 shapes, validator, schema JSON; AC-003 flat-arg allow-list and stateless dispatch; AC-004 workflow pre-check and revert; the pre-check is advisory like the plugin pre-check: it reports the missing items with the exact gate message as a warning and never refuses a close; a server gate refusal is surfaced with the server's message and missing items (`ReplPrecheckAdvisoryWarnsMissingItemsNeverRefuses`). [S6]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-005 PowerShell plugin core. AC-001 `Protect-ReplLosslessScalars` (the core's copy of the `ScoreLexeme` rule across the language boundary, kept in step by the golden contract); AC-002 `Invoke-WorkflowRecordReview` (no failsafe, including in a host session with `unsupportedSince`, where a review is refused and recorded after the drain through the original pair, FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001; every retryable answer, a transport outage, 503 `backend_unavailable`, a busy store's 503 `persistence_error` with `retryable: true`, or 409 `conflict` with `retryable: true`, is reported `persisted: false` with its code and `retryable: true`, exit 1, never `queued`, FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC005); AC-003 `ConvertFrom-McpHvReceipt` (process ConvertHvReceiptToReview, architecture 5.4: sets `hvVerdict` AGREE only when the receipt's OverallVerdict is AGREE and every claim is PASS, sends no `status`, never forwards `hvReceiptPath`); AC-004 advisory local pre-check that warns and never refuses (FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC002) and the stop-gate finding for a close the server refused with `session_turn_completion_gate` (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC003). [S8]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-006 Status lifecycle and review validation. AC-001 `ReviewDefaults` (namespace `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Reviews`, `src/QBrainAi.Services/Reviews/`, architecture 3.5.4) gives the default status for a kind and class and is applied at turn insert, by a section reset, by the Chore flip of policy RequestClassification (only a record still at a default status, only on a turn nonterminal before the write), and by the S3 backfill; AC-002 policy ReviewValidation (architecture 5.3), implemented by the S1 review validator seam, which calls `ScoreVerdict` for every derivation and holds no threshold comparison of its own, validates every review record: the RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand write (origin Agent) and each review a turn body carries through `TurnWritePipeline` (origins Agent, Import, and Replication), before policies RequestClassification and CompletionGate, returning the first violation in its fixed order. [S1 +S4]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007 Thresholds. AC-001 the threshold provider `IReviewThresholdProvider` (architecture 5.3, "Policy seams (S1)": a TurnReviews server type in `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Reviews`) with per-instance overrides through `McpInstanceResolver`; AC-002 `ScoreVerdict` (namespace `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Scoring`, `src/QBrainAi.Services/Scoring/`, architecture 3.5.4) derives `Pass` or `Fail` from both scores against their thresholds, inclusive, and returns the thresholds in force for stamping; Phase B reviews use the configured thresholds in [0,1] with no floor (the agent-context floor of 0.98 is the agent-context domain's); AC-003 startup validation rejects thresholds outside [0,1], first covered in S4 (RED `ReviewThresholdsOutsideZeroToOneStartupRejects`); AC-004 defaults declared under the canonical root `QBrainAi: SessionLog: ReviewThresholds:` in `appsettings.yaml` and read as `Mcp:SessionLog:ReviewThresholds:*` (FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-007-AC001). The `ConfigValidator` check under both roots, canonical first (`./build.ps1 ValidateConfig`, after BUG-BUILD-005), is Nuke code and lives in TR-MCP-NUKE-004, citing TR-MCP-NUKE-008. [S1 +S4]

#### Testing requirements

- TEST-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001 Review storage and graph paths. Tests: provider model tests, `AddSessionLogTurnReviewsAndRequestClassMigrationTests` (SQLite Up/Down/backfill), 28-digit apply test, PostgreSQL and SQL Server integration tests (G3, `./build.ps1 MigrationIntegrationTests --skip InstallTestDependencies`), `SessionLogTurnReviewGraphPathTests`, and the creation-default and reset cases of `SessionLogTurnReviewServiceTests` (S4). Validates FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC002..AC004, -AC006, FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC001 (storage hops), TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001 AC-001..AC-004. Also validates the creation default of FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC002 (case `SessionLogTurnReviewServiceTests.TurnInsertedWithClassChoreHostileUnnecessaryAgentUnstarted`), the reset statuses of FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC004 (case `SessionLogTurnReviewServiceTests.SectionResetCanceledCancelledOrClosedTurnResetsCompletedOrFailedRefused`), and the soft-deleted backfill and the rollback default of FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC006 (cases `BackfillSoftDeletedRowsUnclassifiedAndUnstartedAfterRevive` and `RollbackPriorBinaryInsertsTurnRollForwardReadsDefaults`, S3; the request-class domain names the same two cases for FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-001-AC004). Also validates the reason rows' root ids of TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001 AC-002 (case `ReviewReasonRootIdsEqualItsTurnKey`, at TurnReviews' gate, architecture 8.4) and the database defaults and three-provider migration round trip of TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001 AC-003 (cases `RollbackPriorBinaryInsertsTurnRollForwardReadsDefaults`, `MigrationsUpDownUpThreeProvidersSchemaAndDataEqual`, architecture 8.5).
- TEST-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002 DTOs, converters, sanitizer, read surfaces. Tests: `UnifiedSessionLogDtoContractTests`, `SessionLogDtoParityTests` (the two develop definitions of `UnifiedRequestEntryDto` only; the new review DTOs have one definition), client source-gen round trip, `ScoreLexemeTests` and `ScoreLexemeJsonConverterTests` (each component's one test class, named for it, architecture 3.5 and 8.4), `UtcTimestampJsonConverterContractTests`, `SessionLogSanitizerReviewTests`. Validates FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC001, -AC005, FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003-AC006 (converter side), FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC001 (client JSON hop), -AC002, TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002 AC-001..AC-005. Also validates FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-001-AC005 and updated FR-MCP-SESSIONLOGSAN-001 ac-1 and ac-6 (cases `SessionLogSanitizerReviewTests.ReviewTextFieldsRedactedOnEveryReadSurface`, `SessionLogSanitizerReviewTests.RecordReviewSecretInReasonsStoredRawResponseAndReadsRedacted`) and updated TR-MCP-SESSIONLOGSAN-001 ac-7 (case `SessionLogSanitizerReviewTests.SanitizerReviewPathsCallSecretRedactorSecondPassUnchanged`). Also validates the search text of TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002 AC-005 (case `SessionLogSanitizerReviewTests.SearchRawReviewValueMatchesReadsRedactedReplacedReasonNoMatch`, architecture 3.4 Search). Also validates updated TR-MCP-DTO-001-AC001 (case `TurnDtoClassAndReviewsServerAndClient`) and FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-004-AC003 on the converter (case `ScoreConverterRawLexemeRulesSameCodes`).
- TEST-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003 Review units, sections, client, MCP tool. Tests: `ReviewCommandHandlerTests` (the RecordSessionTurnReviewCommand and ResetSessionTurnReviewCommand handlers against mocked Phase 2 seams), `SessionLogRecordReviewToolTests`, `McpToolDescriptorParityTests` review cases, `McpErrorClassifierReviewTests` (review codes through the one `ClassifiedErrorException` branch), client `SessionLogClientReviewTests` (`MockHttpHandler`), integration `SessionLogReviewControllerTests` (G3, `./build.ps1 Test --test-projects QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.IntegrationTests`), and the reset-versus-complete forced interleaving (G3, `./build.ps1 MigrationIntegrationTests --skip InstallTestDependencies`, S10). Contributes `SessionLogRecordReviewToolTests.SectionReplaceAndClearAgentAndHostile` and `SessionLogClientReviewTests.RecordAndClearTurnReviewSectionSemantics` (the MCP tool and client surfaces) to FR-SUPPORT-016 `ac-5` (owner TEST-SUPPORT-024). Also validates the MCP section tools of FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC001 and TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003 AC-003 (case `McpSectionToolsReviewSectionDispatchReviewUnits`, which it also contributes to TR-SUPPORT-CORE-016 `ac-2`, owner TEST-SUPPORT-024) and their descriptions (case `McpToolDescriptorParityTests.SectionToolDescriptionsListReviewSections`), the MCP item refusal of FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC004 (case `McpDeleteItemReviewSectionRefusedLikeRestItemRoute`), the non-plugin 404 of FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC002 (case `RecordReviewUnknownPairNonPluginSurfaces404NotFound`), and the write isolation of FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-002-AC005 and TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-003 AC-005 (cases `ReviewCommandHandlerTests.ReviewWriteBoundExhausted409ConflictRetryableNothingSaved`, and `TurnWriteForcedInterleavingReviewResetVsCompleteNeverClosedWithoutReview` on SQLite, PostgreSQL, and SQL Server at the gate of TurnReviews' CompletionGate enforcement change set, architecture 8.4). ### Domain: Plugin core runners and host adoption

Bounded contexts: PluginCorePowerShell and HostPluginAdoption (architecture section 8.3). The nine-entry marker `agent_plugins` contracts are the A9 server part that SessionTurnLogging owns (`MarkerFileService.cs`, section 8.3), and the plugin sync with its PowerShell-only checks is BuildTooling's (TR-MCP-NUKE-003, build-tooling domain). Architecture: section 3.1 (the PowerShell-only ruling, the governed-host protections, D6, D7, D8, D11), section 3.2 (host binding table, host limits recorded as feature-matrix rows, TypeScript-host adapter contract), section 3.5.6 (plugin core functions), sections 8.3 to 8.5 (contexts, gates, rollout). Use cases: the host steps of UC-1 to UC-9.

#### Functional requirements

FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 All agent plugins enforce the turn rules.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC001 The TypeScript hosts (cline, cline-v2, opencode) get every plugin behavior of this plan from the PowerShell core through the logic-free adapter (operator, 2026-10-11, PowerShell-only ruling): their host bindings satisfy exactly the ACs in the A8 allocation, including the stop-equivalent rules FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001, -AC002, -AC004, and -AC006 through cline-v2 `afterRun` and opencode `session.idle` through the `event` hook (in A8 driven through recorded host payload fixtures; the host repos run them in A9), and the adapter adds no behavior of its own: it forwards each host event the host binding table names (on opencode's `event` hook only an event of type `session.idle`) and each tool call to the PowerShell core and returns the core's result unchanged, so the structured failure cause (C9) of every core verb reaches the host as the core reports it. On a host whose stop-equivalent cannot hold the run open (P0.2 records it per host; architecture section 3.2, host limits (1), and process EvaluateStopGate), a block finding is recorded through `Update-TurnState` as `stop_gate_overridden_by_host` with the open turn ids and warned at the next prompt hook, because these hosts have no SessionStart event, and the feature matrix records the limitation (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC006; `TsHostStopEquivalentCannotBlockRecordsOverriddenByHostWarnsAtNextPrompt`). The required-memory block these hosts inject today (FR-MCP-MEMORY-007) comes from the PowerShell core: on cline-v2 through `beforeModel` and on opencode on the `chat.message` output, both forwarded by the adapter, and on classic cline as the `context_modification` output of its native UserPromptSubmit PowerShell hook (architecture section 3.2, host limits (3); `ClineUserPromptSubmitReturnsRequiredMemoriesAsContextModification`). The PowerShell core also carries the governed-host protections the approved plan gave the Node core (operator, 2026-10-12, round-2 finding R2-F10, verbatim answer "Port to PowerShell (Recommended)"): every core verb keeps the structured failure cause (`SessionVerbPreservesStructuredCauseC9`); a governed context uses only its bound REPL bridge, never an ambient HTTP fallback, and a missing bridge is a typed final failure (`GovernedContextNoAmbientHttpFallback`); a governed flush uses only the context-bound durable failsafe, keeps its pending, rejected, and quarantined completion blockers, and never replays a global or foreign-workspace failsafe; suppressing a flush is not audit clearance (`GovernedFlushContextBoundFailsafeOnlyNoForeignReplay`). [A8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC002 cline, cline-v2, and opencode adopt the TypeScript-host adapter over the PowerShell core and pass their Pester suites; their jest suites (`jest.config.js` and `tests/*.test.ts`) are removed in the same change, because Pester is the only plugin test runner (architecture section 3.1; TR-MCP-NUKE-013 fails a selected repo that keeps a jest test command). [A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC003 claude-code, claude-cowork, codex, copilot, grok, and grok-bot receive the synced PowerShell core and pass their Pester suites (their bats cases were ported to Pester in P0.1b). [A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC004 Every host either has an arrival mechanism listed in the host binding table (operator D6) or reports `turn_logging_unsupported` on every session-log verb and writes nothing; no host relies on the model to open turns (cline-v2 is supported only when P0.2 shows one run per user message, and classic cline only for a task whose hook payload's `workspaceRoots` holds exactly one root, which is the mint's workspace, while a task with several roots is `turn_logging_unsupported` with zero mints and zero writes; architecture section 3.1, round-5 resolutions and "Host details"; P0.2 records the field; `ClineSingleWorkspaceRootIsMintWorkspace`, `ClineSeveralWorkspaceRootsTurnLoggingUnsupportedZeroMintsZeroWrites` in A8). A hook source that the copilot or classic-cline single-registration check cannot read (permissions, a policy directory) counts as a failing check, because the check must prove exactly one MCP prompt hook across every source (D11; `ClineUnreadableHookSourceFailsClosedTurnLoggingUnsupported` in A8, `CopilotUnreadableHookSourceFailsClosedTurnLoggingUnsupported` in A9). While the copilot or classic-cline duplicate check fails, every session-log verb of that plugin, not only its prompt hook, reports `turn_logging_unsupported` and writes nothing to the server; when the check starts failing in a host session after one of its requests was delivered, the first verb or hook that observes it records `unsupportedSince` with reason `duplicate_prompt_hook`, and writes for a turn already open go to the plugin failsafe (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001, `ClineDuplicateHookAfterDeliveryUnsupportedSinceOpenTurnCloseCaptured` for classic cline in A8, `DuplicateHookAfterDeliveryUnsupportedSinceOpenTurnCloseCaptured` for copilot in A9). [A8 +A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC005 All nine plugin versions are bumped to the one sync version, 1.120.0 (BUG-BUILD-008 lands at 1.119.0 first; architecture sections 8.4 and 8.5), and reported correctly by the marker `agent_plugins` section after a server restart. The marker generator (`MarkerFileService.BuildDefaultAgentPlugins`, `src/QBrainAi.Services/Services/MarkerFileService.cs:532-611` at `a8d3934b`, five entries today) gains four contracts: `ClaudeCowork` (mcpserver-claude-cowork-plugin), `GrokBot` (mcpserver-grok-bot-plugin), `ClineV2` (mcpserver-cline-v2-plugin), and `OpenCode` (mcpserver-opencode-plugin), each with source type, plugin name, version read from that plugin's manifest, activation, startup command, required environment, hook and tool expectations, and root hints in the same shape as the existing five; P0.7 confirms each plugin's `PLUGIN_AGENT_NAME` and source type from its repository and records any difference as a RequirementRefined decision before A9; a marker read back after restart lists all nine with their bumped versions (`MarkerFileServiceAgentPluginsNineEntriesVersionsFromManifests`). The signed `agentPlugins.contractDigest` (`ComputeAgentPluginsDigest`, `MarkerFileService.cs:757-777` at `a8d3934b`, which hashes ten fields of every contract) then covers all nine contracts, so the digest value and the marker signature value change, while the signed field list (`SignaturePayloadFields`, `:50-83`) and the payload format of TR-MCP-SEC-005 stay as they are (`MarkerContractDigestCoversNineContractsSignedFieldListUnchanged`). [A9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC006 `docs/AGENT-PLUGIN-FEATURE-MATRIX.md` lists, for each of the nine plugins and each turn-logging feature (ingress mint, arrival bridge or adapter, host-context binding, stop gate, session end), whether it is supported, adapted, or a documented capability limitation (D7) with the reason and the P0.2 receipt; every `turn_logging_unsupported` host appears there as a limitation. P0.8 writes it by restructuring today's eight-plugin feature grid (`docs/AGENT-PLUGIN-FEATURE-MATRIX.md:20-46` at `a8d3934b`: no grok-bot, no receipts) into one row per plugin and feature, keeping its existing feature rows and bringing its "Primary Implementation" and "Test Framework" rows (`:42-43`, which name bash, Node, bats, and jest) to the PowerShell-only state. The host limits of architecture section 3.2 are rows, each with its P0.2 receipt: (1) stop gate: classic cline has no stop-equivalent, so the gate never runs there and its host sessions end only by the 12-hour idle rule, and cline-v2 `afterRun` and opencode `session.idle` evaluate and report but cannot hold the run open (`stop_gate_overridden_by_host`, AC001); (2) per-tool audit: supported on cline-v2 (`beforeTool`, `afterTool`) and opencode (`tool.execute.before`, `.after`) through the core's `tool-audit` hook, which keeps the `tool_call` and `tool_result` dialog items they write today and writes no `design_decision` action (architecture section 3.2, Tool-audit hook; TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 AC-013); the PowerShell hosts have no tool audit, as today, and QB-AGENT-001's `tool_execution` work is separate (C6); (3) required memories (FR-MCP-MEMORY-007): cline-v2 through `beforeModel`, opencode on the `chat.message` output, classic cline through its native hook's `context_modification`, with the explicit memory-list fallback where P0.2 shows that context does not reach the model; (4) classic cline tasks with several `workspaceRoots`: `turn_logging_unsupported` (AC004; architecture section 3.1, "Host details"); and session end on codex through the `session-end` wrapper the generator writes for codex's SessionEnd entry (TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-009 AC-005). A host whose queued messages reach no arrival mechanism at enqueue is `turn_logging_unsupported` (D6; operator D5 requires minting at enqueue) with zero mints and writes until an enqueue adapter exists, and its row states that outcome; a P0.2 probe with outcome `unavailable` selects the row's stated default where one exists, and for an arrival mechanism selects D6 `turn_logging_unsupported` with reason "P0.2 unavailable", shown to the operator. The claude-code and claude-cowork rows also record the D6 limitation that an unattributable queue record (equal-content queued messages with no shared key) fails that host session closed for the rest of the session (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-001-AC010). [A9]

FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008 All agent plugins expose class and reviews.
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC001 The TypeScript hosts get `requestClass`, `recordReview`, decimal-string scores, the completion pre-check, and HV mapping from the PowerShell core (S8) through `lib/tool-descriptors.json` and their adapters. [S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC002 cline, cline-v2, and opencode adopt them and pass their Pester suites. [S9]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC003 The six PowerShell hosts receive the synced core with updated skill docs and pass their suites. [S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC004 One golden contract file `docs/context/session-turn-review-contract.json` drives the C# and Pester rule tests. Its plugin pre-check cases include the unsupported-session failsafe close and the advisory behavior (warns with the missing items, never refuses) of FR-MCP-SESSIONCLASS-004-AC002, run by Pester only (the server has no host session state). [S1 +S8]
- FR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008-AC005 The feature matrix (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC006) also lists `requestClass`, `recordReview`, exact scores, and the completion pre-check per plugin, with any documented limitation. [S11]

#### Technical requirements

- TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 TypeScript-host bindings and the adapter contract in the PowerShell core (`plugins/core/lib-ps`; operator, 2026-10-11, PowerShell-only ruling; architecture section 3.2). AC-001 the cline, cline-v2, and opencode rows of the host binding table (nonces, host session keys, child-session marker, the `cl-` equal-field rule applied under the host session lock before the mint) are implemented in the PowerShell functions A3 and A4 own, and classic cline's native UserPromptSubmit command hook (`pwsh -NoLogo -NoProfile -NonInteractive -File <plugin root>/lib/plugin-hook.ps1 -HookName user-prompt-submit -HostName cline`) mints with the `cl-` nonce and the `taskId` key, takes the mint's workspace from the payload's `workspaceRoots` when it holds exactly one root and reports `turn_logging_unsupported` for a task with several (architecture section 3.1, "Host details"), and returns the required-memory block as the hook's `context_modification` output (`apps/vscode/proto/cline/hooks.proto:37-41` in the local Cline source at `b090c4a08`); AC-002 on hosts without a SessionStart event the first ingress event records the host session's `mcpSessionId` in `turn-state.yaml` under the host session lock before its mint; AC-003 the stop-equivalents (cline-v2 `afterRun`, opencode `session.idle` delivered through the `event` hook) run A7's Stop gate and never close a turn, and where the host's P0.2 row records that its stop-equivalent cannot hold the run open, a block finding is recorded as `stop_gate_overridden_by_host` with the open turn ids and warned at the next prompt hook (process EvaluateStopGate); AC-004 `lib/tool-descriptors.json` lists every tool the TypeScript hosts expose (the tools `plugins/core/lib-node/src/tools` exposes today, which P0.7 records; a tool with no PowerShell method is ported to one first), each with the `method` it runs through `Invoke-McpPlugin.ps1 -Command Invoke` and its `mutates` flag, with review scores declared as strings; AC-005 each TypeScript host's adapter forwards each host hook the host binding table names (cline-v2 `beforeRun`, `beforeModel`, `beforeTool`, `afterTool`, `afterRun`; opencode `chat.message`, `tool.execute.before`, `tool.execute.after`, `event`, `shell.env`, `experimental.chat.messages.transform`), as an argument list and never a shell string, to `lib/plugin-hook.ps1 -HostEvent <hook> -HostName <host> -WorkspacePath <the workspace directory the host's plugin API reports, recorded per host in P0.7>` with `{ input, output }` on stdin unchanged, returns a hook result where the host API takes a return object (cline-v2) as the host runtime's JSON parse of the object the core writes, never modified, and copies each property of the `output` object the core returns onto the host's output argument where the hook has one (opencode), forwards opencode's `event` hook only for an event whose `type` is `session.idle` and returns at once for any other type without starting `pwsh` (`OpenCodeEventHookOtherEventTypeNotForwardedNoPwshStart`), registers exactly the descriptors, forwards each tool call to `lib/Invoke-McpPlugin.ps1 -Command Invoke -Method <method> -WorkspacePath <dir> -InvocationFromStdin` with `{ params, hostContext }` on stdin, returns the core's stdout and exit status unchanged, returns the fixed failure `plugin_core_unavailable` when `pwsh` cannot be started or the event cannot be serialized, delivers tool arguments containing quotes, Unicode, and newlines to the core exactly on Linux and on Windows, and holds no other logic beyond its one fixed host-binding constant, the `session.idle` event type; AC-006 a write captured as `unsupported_session` on these hosts goes to the failsafe only and reaches no persistence path before its drain (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-005-AC001); AC-007 the alias journal component of architecture 3.5.6 (`sessions/<key>/aliases.yaml`) provides its consumer functions `Register-McpAliasConsumer`, `Get-McpUnacknowledgedAlias`, `Get-McpAlias`, and `Confirm-McpAliasAcknowledged` with the data model (permanent `lookup` entries, prunable `delivery` entries whose required set is fixed at the alias's commit), return values, errors, host-session binding, atomicity, and replay rule stated there, and no other alias interface exists; the TypeScript `createAliasService` interface agreed on 2026-10-09 is withdrawn by the ruling, and this PowerShell interface is posted to QB-AGENT-001 as a CONTRACT entry (no exactly-once delivery claim); AC-008 `plugin-hook.ps1 -HostEvent <name> -HostName <host>` maps each host event the host binding table names to its canonical hook (cline-v2 `beforeRun` and opencode `chat.message` to `user-prompt-submit`, whose returned opencode `output` also carries the `REQUIRED MEMORIES` block of FR-MCP-MEMORY-007, cline-v2 `beforeModel` to the required-memory handler, which returns the model request with that block added unless the request already carries it, and in `Stateless` mode, where cline-v2's P0.2 row (probe (j)) records that the returned request replaces the model's messages, also applies the history-exclusion handler of RunStatelessInvocation (both render the block with the one core renderer `plugins/core/lib-ps/memory-context.ps1`, architecture section 3.1, "Required-memory renderer"), cline-v2 `afterRun` to `stop-gate`, cline-v2 `beforeTool` and `afterTool` and opencode `tool.execute.before` and `tool.execute.after` to `tool-audit` with phase `before` or `after` (AC-013), opencode `event` to `stop-gate` when `event.type` is `session.idle` (session key `event.properties.sessionID`) and to an exit-0 no-op for every other event type (a defense, because the adapter forwards no other type), opencode `shell.env` to the shell-environment handler that returns `MCP_HOST_SESSION_KEY` from `input.sessionID`, and opencode `experimental.chat.messages.transform` to the history-exclusion handler of RunStatelessInvocation) and refuses any other event with `host_event_unknown`, writing nothing; AC-009 `Invoke-McpPlugin.ps1 -InvocationFromStdin` reads `{ params, hostContext }` as UTF-8 JSON from stdin, binds `params` exactly as `-ParamsObject` would, and derives the host session key from `hostContext` per the host binding table (opencode `sessionID`, cline-v2 `conversationId`), never from `params` (`TsAdapterTwoSessionsIdenticalArgsDistinctHostSessionKeys`), and both it and `plugin-hook.ps1 -HostEvent` read stdin as UTF-8 regardless of the console code page, Windows included (`CoreStdinUtf8IndependentOfConsoleCodePage`, run on Linux and on Windows); AC-010 every core verb keeps the structured failure cause of a response (detail code and retryable flag, `classified-error.ps1`) and never reduces it to a string, so C9 detail codes such as `mint_collision_exhausted`, and a 409 `conflict` with `retryable: true`, which the core handles as an outage (architecture section 3.1, "Deadlock and busy outcomes"), reach the caller with their flags; AC-011 a governed context (a host-selected, absolute REPL executable bound to the invocation context) uses only that bound bridge and never an ambient HTTP or REST fallback for its verbs (session log, TODO, requirements, triage), and a missing bridge is a typed final failure; the marker trust check's read-only health and nonce request (`plugins/core/lib-ps/marker-resolver.ps1:218` at `a8d3934b`) is not a verb fallback and stays (architecture section 3.1, "Governed verbs and the trust check"), so `GovernedContextNoAmbientHttpFallback` drives the verbs only; AC-012 a governed flush uses only the context-bound durable failsafe, keeps its pending, rejected, and quarantined completion blockers (C3, C8), and never replays a global or foreign-workspace failsafe directory through a governed bridge; suppressing a flush is not audit clearance (AC-010..AC-012 (operator, 2026-10-12, round-2 finding R2-F10, verbatim answer "Port to PowerShell (Recommended)")); AC-013 the tool-audit hook of architecture section 3.2 (Tool-audit hook): the four tool hooks of AC-005 reach `tool-audit`, which reads the tool name, input, and error per host as the host plugins do today and appends the `tool_call` or `tool_result` dialog item the Node core writes today (`plugins/core/lib-node/src/runtime/host-context.ts:355-385` at `a8d3934b`) to the host session's write target as a hook invocation, never model evidence and with no `design_decision` action; it writes nothing without a write target or in an unsupported host session, keeps an outage's write in the failsafe, and always answers exit 0 with an empty result, so a tool call is never blocked. [A8]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-009 Per-host adoption (HostPluginAdoption, with the A9 server part of SessionTurnLogging and the wrapper generator of PluginCorePowerShell; architecture sections 3.2 and 8.3). AC-001 host glue per plugin, per the host binding table: codex `hooks/hooks.json` (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse, Stop, SessionEnd) whose SessionEnd entry calls the generated `session-end` wrapper, with the operator's hook trust step and `turn_logging_unsupported` until the codex live assertion passes; copilot `plugin.json` pointed at `hooks/hooks.json`, the dangling `.sh` hook file removed, and the single-registration check; claude-cowork's connector repair, which replaces the dangling `bin/mcpserver-stdio.cmd` that its `.mcp.json` names (`.mcp.json:4` at `03e934f`) with a PowerShell script started as `pwsh -NoLogo -NoProfile -NonInteractive -File ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/bin/mcpserver-stdio.ps1`, its target recorded in Cowork's P0.2 row and no `.cmd` left (architecture section 3.1, "Host details"; `CoworkMcpJsonStartsPowerShellConnectorNoCmdFile`), and its bridge adoption only when its P0.2 bridge probes pass; grok and grok-bot hook registrations re-checked against the synced core; the TypeScript hosts' adapter entry files (for classic cline the stdio MCP entry plus its native UserPromptSubmit PowerShell command hook, installed where P0.2 records Cline's hook scripts); and the `GrokBot` host kind in `tests/QBrainAi.PluginIntegration.Tests` (`PluginHostKind.cs:6` and `PluginHostProcessAdapter.IsPowerShellHost`, `PluginHostProcessAdapter.cs:180-185` at `a8d3934b`); AC-002 plugin docs, skills, ENFORCEMENT.md, and their tests updated (the codex `skills/enforcement/SKILL.md` loses its manual prompt-hook steps); each host's memory skill loads the synced core renderer `lib/memory-context.ps1` and the host-repo copies of `hooks/scripts/memory-context.ps1` are removed (architecture section 3.1, "Required-memory renderer"; `MemorySkillLoadsCoreRendererNoHostCopy` in every plugin's suite); and each TypeScript host's jest suites replaced by Pester: the adapter suite `tests/TypeScriptHostAdapter.Tests.ps1`, the AC-SKILLS cases of TEST-MCP-150 and the clear-session case `ClearSessionSkillListsSelectionAndGateSteps` (TEST-MCP-CLEARSESSION-001, turn-persistence domain) in `tests/Skills.Tests.ps1`, and the memory-skill cases of FR-MCP-MEMORY-007 in `tests/MemorySkill.Tests.ps1`; AC-003 version bumps to the one sync version, 1.120.0 (architecture section 8.5); AC-004 nine marker `agent_plugins` contracts generated by `MarkerFileService.BuildDefaultAgentPlugins` with a nine-entry readback test, and the signed contract digest covering all nine with the signed field list unchanged (FR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008-AC005); AC-005 the wrapper generator `plugins/core/hooks-templates/generate-wrappers.ps1` (PluginCorePowerShell's file, its output checked in G4) writes the codex `session-end` wrapper, which its codex branch does not write today (`:24-34` at `a8d3934b`), and accepts `grok-bot`, which its `ValidateSet` lacks (`:8`), and writes its wrappers (`GenerateWrappersCodexWritesSessionEndWrapper`, `GenerateWrappersGrokBotWritesHookWrappers`). The sync itself (`SyncAgentPlugins`) is Nuke code and lives in TR-MCP-NUKE-003. [A9]
- TR-MCP-SESSIONREVIEW-008 Review surfaces on the TypeScript hosts through the PowerShell core. AC-001 review scores reach the server as their original text through each adapter; AC-002 the `session_record_review` and `requestClass` descriptors map to the S8 commands; AC-003 the stop-equivalents block on a server gate refusal, naming the server's missing items (a host whose stop-equivalent cannot hold the run open records every block finding, this one included, under TR-MCP-SESSIONTURN-008 AC-003). [S9]

In `Pilot` and `Stateless` the block is injected as at any request boundary, and in `Stateless` mode the record holds with no text change: the model input is the rendered package, the current request, and the host's deterministic `REQUIRED MEMORIES` block, with earlier requests' messages excluded (FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-009), and on each later model call of the same request (a tool loop) the block stays with the request's own messages (architecture section 3.1, "Memories in Stateless mode" and "Tool loops in `Stateless`", and section 6.1; asserted by `StatelessHostSentinelPriorTurnAbsentFromModelRequest` and `StatelessToolLoopOwnToolCallsKeptPriorRequestsExcluded`, cases of the agent-surfaces domain's FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-009 TEST). Every host renders the block with the one core renderer `plugins/core/lib-ps/memory-context.ps1`, reconciled in P0.1 from the claude-cowork (512 lines at `03e934f`) and opencode (436 lines at `36ed34d`) copies of `hooks/scripts/memory-context.ps1` and restored by P0.1 at that path in the six host repos whose memory skill and tests load it without having it (claude-code, codex, copilot, grok, grok-bot, and classic cline; their baseline fix), then moved into the core by PluginCorePowerShell in Phase 4, synced to all nine, and the host copies removed by HostPluginAdoption (architecture section 3.1, "Required-memory renderer"). #### Test churn

Existing tests whose outcome this domain changes, at `a8d3934b` for McpServer and, for the plugin repos, at the heads read on 2026-10-13 (claude-code `472340b`, claude-cowork `03e934f`, codex `2237b61`, copilot `b54e029`, grok `6400f6b`, grok-bot `d7074c5`, cline `7909599`, cline-v2 `e776d94`, opencode `36ed34d`); each change is carried by the change that causes it, never by an exclusion (architecture section 8.4).

- Bats suites of the six PowerShell hosts (P0.1b, `MAINT-BATSTOPESTER-001`): 450 cases (claude-code `memory.bats` 5, `skills.bats` 94, `smoke.bats` 4; claude-cowork 5, 92, 4; codex `manifest.bats` 11, `memory.bats` 5, `smoke.bats` 3; copilot `memory.bats` 5, `plan-output.bats` 6, `skills.bats` 9, `smoke.bats` 3; grok and grok-bot 5, 94, 3 each) move to Pester in each repo's `tests/*.Tests.ps1` with their outcomes unchanged, and the `.bats` files are deleted (TR-MCP-NUKE-013 fails a repo that keeps one). ### Domain: Context packages and token budget

Bounded contexts (architecture 8.3): ContextPackages (the four package units, `SourceResolver` and `SourceKind`, `PackageAssembler`, `CandidateRanker`, `BuiltPackageLookup`), StorageSchema (the package entities and the `<utc>_AddAgentContext` migration), and the Phase 2 shared files (`AgentContextOptions`, the package DTOs, the surfaces of FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-008). Use case: architecture 7.11. Scope entries: AgentContextContracts, AgentContextStorage, AgentContextPackages (90-tail "Phase C allocation").

Operator decision "Outline now, catalog in P0" (catalog rules above): each FR below is an outline statement aligned with the architecture; P0 writes every AC of FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-001 and -002 and of their TR from architecture sections 6.2 to 6.7, and "Named cases for the P0 catalog" below is the case list P0 starts from.

#### Functional requirements

- FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-001 Context package construction and provenance: for the session-log turn named by `agent`, `sessionId`, and `requestId` (TurnLocator), the server builds a bounded, reproducible package from the request, the canonical profile and rules (`ProfileSources`, `RuleSources`), the applicable requirements (those linked to the turn's TODO, an `ISSUE-<n>` id included, plus those linked to the turn by relationships or named in `focusRefs`; a turn whose `todoId` is `None` has only the latter two, architecture 3.1, Applicable requirements), related turns (never the request's own turn), TODOs, receipts, trusted fresh prior responses, and code at the workspace HEAD commit; it records each item's source hash over the stored normal form (ContentHasher), the commit, the selection rationale, token usage, contradiction and blocker findings, every omitted candidate with its reason, and an immutable input snapshot; it redacts secrets before anything is stored (SecretRedactor); it links the package `derived_from` each included source and `relevant_to` its turn in the package's own transaction, and those links are protected (a Rule file outside version control is provenance only, never a link or a staleness dependency); and it replays the package identically from that snapshot only, never from live sources, configuration, relationships, or response statuses. A repeated idempotency key with equal client inputs returns the stored package before any source is read, and with other inputs is 409 `conflict` with `retryable: false`. Packages are readable in every Mode. The record states, as a glossary entry, that a context package is not FR-SUPPORT-010's context pack (ranked context chunks for a query) and cites FR-MCP-082's bounded TODO execution context as related and not reused (architecture 6.1). Units: BuildAgentContextPackageCommand, GetAgentContextPackageQuery, QueryAgentContextPackagesQuery, ReplayAgentContextPackageQuery (architecture 6.5).
- FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-002 Token budget and explicit overflow: token counts are the deterministic estimate `ceil(utf8ByteCount / BytesPerToken)` per item, and the request's optional `tokenBudget` (from 1024 up to the configured `TokenBudget`) narrows the budget; mandatory context (the request, profile, rules, and applicable requirements) is never truncated and never cut by the candidate limits; mandatory tokens equal to the budget build, and one token more fails the build with 422 `agent_context_budget_exceeded` (`details { tokenBudget, mandatoryTokenCount, overflowTokenCount, mandatoryItems }`) and stores an `Overflow` package with its mandatory items, token counts, and input snapshot and no content; optional candidates fill the remaining budget in rank order (a candidate whose tokens equal the remainder is included), each kind capped at `MaxCandidatesPerKind` (`PriorResponse` at `MaxPriorResponses`), the rest omitted with reason `budget` or `below_candidate_limit`; a repeated or concurrent build with the same key and client inputs returns the same 422 with the stored diagnostics, never a package; in `Pilot` and `Stateless` the failure fails the invocation (FR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-009). Unit: BuildAgentContextPackageCommand with the nested activity FillBudgetWithCandidates (architecture 6.2, 6.5).

#### Technical requirements

- TR-MCP-AGENTCONTEXT-001 Package construction, replay, and package reads. Scope for P0's ACs: the record and handler files (one type per file, MA0048) of BuildAgentContextPackageCommand in `src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Packages/Commands/` (namespace `QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.AgentContext.Packages.Commands`) and of GetAgentContextPackageQuery, QueryAgentContextPackagesQuery, and ReplayAgentContextPackageQuery in `src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Packages/Queries/` (`QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.AgentContext.Packages.Queries`); the ContextPackages components `SourceResolver` and `SourceKind` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Sources/`; `ReadCommit`, the canonical hash per kind, the wire aliases `Code` and `File` of `CodeFile` and `Response` of `PriorResponse`) and `PackageAssembler`, `CandidateRanker`, and `BuiltPackageLookup` (`src/QBrainAi.Services/AgentContext/Packages/`), each with one test class (architecture 3.5, 3.5.5); the entities `AgentContextPackageEntity`, `AgentContextPackageItemEntity`, `AgentContextPackageFocusRefEntity`, `AgentContextPackageInputEntity`, `AgentContextPackageInputLinkEntity`, `AgentContextOmissionEntity`, and `AgentContextFindingEntity` in `src/QBrainAi.Storage/Entities/AgentContext/` (`QBrainAi.Support.Mcp.Storage.Entities.AgentContext`; composite keys with `WorkspaceId`, root ids on every child row, registered by StorageSchema); the request and result DTOs in `src/QBrainAi.Client/Models/AgentContext/` (`QBrainAi.Client.Models.AgentContext`); keys `pkg-<requestId>-<nn>` from ReadableKeyAllocator; IdempotentCreate with policy `Conflict` over `PayloadSha256` of the client inputs, looked up read-only before any source read; the reads (ReadBuildInputs) before a database-only SerializableWrite whose delegate re-checks the turn status, each attempt under `StorageCommandBudget.Default` (`src/QBrainAi.Storage/StorageCommandBudget.cs:12` at `a8d3934b`), whose expiry is the 503 `backend_unavailable` storage answer; input snapshots and audit rows kept with their package for audit, bounded by the candidate limits and the token budget, with no purge in this plan (architecture 3.1, Storage budget and sizes); RelationshipWriter links with `CreatedBy` `BuildAgentContextPackageCommand`; [AgentContextContracts +AgentContextPackages]

"Plan-named" marks a case this plan names because the architecture draws the branch or lists the code without a case; every other name is the architecture's.
